1	# Architecture
2	
3	How the `vc-x1` binary is structured: the clap-aware CLI
4	layer and the (emerging) clap-free subcommand layer.
5	
6	This is a living document; the app is under continuous
7	development and the repo will keep changing. Per-cycle design
8	discussion lives in `notes/chores-*.md`; near-term tasks in
9	`notes/todo.md`.
10	
11	## Overview
12	
13	`vc-x1` is a single binary that supports subcommands,
14	`vc-x1 <subcommand>`, for managing a jj-git **dual-repo workspace**
15	with an app repo plus a `.claude` bot-session repo. The commits
16	cross-reference each other via `ochid:` trailers at the bottom
17	of each commit message. See [`README.md`](README.md)
18	for the user-facing picture and [`CLAUDE.md`](CLAUDE.md) for
19	the bot workflow.
20	
21	## Two layers: CLI args vs subcommand Context + Params
22	
23	Goal: separate clap-aware argument parsing from subcommand
24	operation logic, so a future front-end (TUI, library
25	embedding) can call the same core without dragging clap
26	along. The trigger was `args.account.account`-style
27	nested-leaf access leaking into subcommand bodies; accessor
28	shortcuts were rejected as hiding the mismatch rather than
29	fixing it.
30	
31	- **CLI layer (clap-aware)**:
32	  Builds `XxxParams` from the user-facing `XxxArgs`
33	  `#[derive(Args)]` clap struct. Options/flags used in more
34	  than one subcommand live as flattened leaves in
35	  `src/options_flags/`.
36	- **Subcommand layer (clap-free)**:
37	  Entrypoint shape: `fn x(ctx: &Context, params: &XxxParams) -> Result<…>`.
38	  And this isolates the subcommand from user and clap.
39	  - `Context` — common state shared with all subcommands.
40	  - `XxxParams` — a subcommand's parameters, derived from
41	    config or command-line arguments.
42	- **Boundary conversion**:
43	  Converting `XxxArgs` to `XxxParams` uses either
44	  `impl From<&XxxArgs> for XxxParams` or
45	  `impl TryFrom<&XxxArgs> for XxxParams`, depending on whether
46	  the conversion can fail.
47	
48	Two parameters per subcommand; the signature documents what
49	the subcommand depends on. `Context` is the same across all
50	subcommands; `XxxParams` carry the per-subcommand inputs.
51	
52	Rules for the subcommand layer (some still aspirational — see
53	[Migration A](#migration-a--args--context--params)):
54	
55	1. Plain options, flat fields, `Default` — no clap types in
56	   `XxxParams`. Domain types (`RepoSelector`, `Scope`) are
57	   fine; leaf wrappers (`RepoOption`) are not.
58	2. Typed errors (`enum XError`, not `Box<dyn Error>`) — a
59	   TUI matches variants to pick dialogs; CLI formats them.
60	3. Returned outcomes, not `println!` — each subcommand
61	   returns a structured result; CLI formats it, a library
62	   writes nothing to stdout itself.
63	4. Progress via an optional `&mut dyn ProgressSink` on
64	   long-running subcommands — CLI installs a stderr sink, tests a
65	   recording sink.
66	5. No globals, no implicit cwd/env reads in subcommands — CLI
67	   resolves cwd/env once at startup and builds `Context`.
68	
69	None of this is sacred — the whole structure is an experiment
70	and the repo keeps changing. The current scope is deliberately
71	narrow:
72	
73	- a small `Context` — two params, not one god blob;
74	- no trait-based DI yet;
75	- one crate, not a workspace split;
76	- `src/options_flags/` leaves left in place — only their
77	  consumers change shape.
78	
79	Widen any of it when the evidence (a second front-end, a second
80	consumer crate, a real platform-surface divergence) calls for
81	it — not before.
82	
83	### Naming
84	
85	Earlier drafts of this design (and the `notes/chores-09.md`
86	capture) used `Workspace` / `XOptions`. The implementation
87	(0.44.0) chose `Context` / `XxxParams` instead:
88	
89	- `Workspace` → `Context` — Cargo owns "workspace" formally,
90	  and this codebase already uses "workspace" for the
91	  dual-repo project root (`find_workspace_root`).
92	- `XOptions` → `XxxParams` — avoids visual collision with
93	  `Option<T>` (a params struct mixes required and
94	  `Option<T>`-typed fields) and with `src/options_flags/`.
95	
96	## Module map
97	
98	CLI layer:
99	
100	- `src/main.rs` — the clap edge:
101	  - `pub struct Cli` — clap `Parser`.
102	  - `pub enum Commands` — clap `Subcommand`; matched in the
103	    dispatch arms.
104	  - `cli_with_banner()` — walks the command tree adding the
105	    `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner as `before_help` on every subcommand.
106	  - `CompleteEnv` wiring for dynamic completion.
107	  - `bm_track` — a permanent `main`-bookmark tracking
108	    diagnostic printed on entry/exit of every command.
109	
110	- `src/options_flags/` — options/flags shared across
111	  subcommands, so a flag is defined once (parser, help,
112	  completer); Migration B tracks what's been moved here so
113	  far. See
114	  [`src/options_flags/README.md`](src/options_flags/README.md).
115	  - reusable **leaves**: `account`, `config`, `dry_run`,
116	    `private`, `push_retry`, `repo`, `scope`, `use_template`.
117	  - reusable **bundles**: `provision_bundle`.
118	- `src/common.rs` — `CommonArgs` (the shared positional-rev /
119	  `-R` repo-list / `-n` / `--limit` / `-L` flag set flattened
120	  by `chid`, `desc`, `list`, `show`) plus the repo-iteration
121	  and revision-resolution helpers they share.
122	
123	Subcommand layer scaffolding:
124	
125	- `src/context.rs` — `Context`: the shared platform handle
126	  every subcommand runs against. Today it carries the loaded
127	  `UserConfig` and the `--log` path; built once at startup
128	  via `Context::load(log)`.
129	- `src/config.rs` — `UserConfig` (`~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml`).
130	- `src/scope.rs` — `Scope` enum (`Roles(Vec<Side>)` for the
131	  dual-repo `code`/`bot` roles, `Single(PathBuf)` for
132	  single-repo mode) and `parse_scope`.
133	
134	Subcommand modules — each holds an `XxxArgs` `#[derive(Args)]`
135	struct and a `pub fn x(...)` entrypoint:
136	
137	| Module | Subcommand | Notes |
138	| --- | --- | --- |
139	| `chid.rs` | `chid` | flattens `CommonArgs` |
140	| `desc.rs` (+ `desc_helpers.rs`) | `desc` | flattens `CommonArgs` |
141	| `list.rs` | `list` | flattens `CommonArgs` |
142	| `show.rs` | `show` | flattens `CommonArgs` |
143	| `validate_desc.rs` | `validate-desc` | |
144	| `fix_desc.rs` | `fix-desc` | |
145	| `clone.rs` | `clone` | |
146	| `init.rs` (+ `init/params.rs`) | `init` | subcommand-layer worked example (0.44.0) |
147	| `symlink.rs` | `symlink` | |
148	| `sync.rs` | `sync` | |
149	| `finalize.rs` | `finalize` | migrated (0.46.0) — `finalize(&Context, &FinalizeParams)`, `TryFrom<&FinalizeArgs>` |
150	| `push.rs` | `push` | resumable state machine |
151	
152	Support:
153	
154	- `src/repo_utils.rs`, `src/url.rs`, `src/toml_simple.rs`,
155	  `src/logging.rs` — jj/repo helpers, URL parsing, a minimal
156	  TOML reader, the CLI logger.
157	- `src/test_helpers.rs`, `src/test_tmp_root.rs` —
158	  `#[cfg(test)]` fixtures (`Fixture` / `FixturePor`) and
159	  tempdir resolution. Subcommand modules also carry their
160	  own `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` (and `integration_tests` for
161	  `sync` / `push`).
162	
163	## Subcommand model
164	
165	Adding a subcommand `x`:
166	
167	1. Create `src/x.rs` with `pub struct XxxArgs`
168	   (`#[derive(Args)]`), composing leaves from
169	   `src/options_flags/` where one exists.
170	2. Add a variant `X(x::XxxArgs)` to `Commands` in
171	   `src/main.rs` with a `///` summary (and
172	   `#[command(long_about = …)]` for the long form).
173	3. Add a dispatch arm in `main()` calling `x::x(&args)` (or,
174	   once ported, `x::x(&ctx, &params)`).
175	4. Doc comments on `#[arg(...)]` fields drive `--help`; add
176	   `#[arg(verbatim_doc_comment, …)]` on any field whose doc
177	   comment uses bullets (clap otherwise reflows them into
178	   prose).
179	5. Dynamic value completion (`--account=<TAB>`) is an
180	   `ArgValueCompleter` attached to the leaf in
181	   `src/options_flags/` — completion is a clap-aware concern
182	   and stays at this layer.
183	
184	## Migration A — args → Context + Params
185	
186	Port each subcommand's `pub fn x(args: &XxxArgs)` to
187	`pub fn x(ctx: &Context, params: &XxxParams)`, adding an
188	`XxxParams` flat struct + a `From` (or `TryFrom`, if the
189	conversion is fallible) at the binary edge. `init` (0.44.0)
190	and `finalize` (0.46.0) are done; the rest follow the same
191	shape. The live checklist is the "Subcommand layer / CLI
192	decoupling" item in [`notes/todo.md`](notes/todo.md).
193	
194	| Subcommand | Status |
195	| --- | --- |
196	| `init` | done (0.44.0) — `init(&Context, &InitParams)`; `From<&InitArgs>` |
197	| `finalize` | done (0.46.0) — `finalize(&Context, &FinalizeParams)`; `TryFrom<&FinalizeArgs>` (fallible boundary); `--log` moved onto `Context` |
198	| `sync` | not started |
199	| `chid` | not started |
200	| `desc` | not started |
201	| `list` | not started |
202	| `show` | not started |
203	| `validate-desc` | not started |
204	| `fix-desc` | not started |
205	| `clone` | not started |
206	| `push` | not started |
207	| `symlink` | not started |
208	
209	The remaining nine are planned as one multi-step cycle
210	(`0.47.0-N`), one step per subcommand (`chid`/`desc`/`list`/
211	`show` ride with the separate "CommonArgs sweep" since their
212	Migration A and B are entangled).
213	
214	Out of scope for the early ports (deferred until a real
215	consumer surfaces): typed errors, returned outcomes vs
216	`println!`, the `ProgressSink`, and `Context` fields beyond
217	`UserConfig` + the `--log` path (`finalize` surfaced `log`).
218	
219	## Migration B — per-subcommand flags → `src/options_flags/`
220	
221	Independently of Migration A, per-subcommand inline `#[arg]`
222	fields are being lifted into reusable leaves/bundles under
223	`src/options_flags/` so a flag is defined (parser, help,
224	completer) exactly once. See
225	[`src/options_flags/README.md`](src/options_flags/README.md)
226	for the leaf / bundle / Pattern-A mechanics and the
227	Flag-vs-Option classification.
228	
229	State today:
230	
231	- **`init`** — fully composed from leaves/bundles
232	  (`account`, `repo`, `scope`, the `provision` bundle,
233	  `use_template`, `config`).
234	- **`chid` / `desc` / `list` / `show`** — share
235	  `common::CommonArgs`, a per-domain shared struct rather
236	  than an `options_flags/` leaf. The "CommonArgs sweep"
237	  todo item folds these into the leaf model (and drops the
238	  repeatable `-R`/`--repo` in favor of the `--scope` path
239	  form).
240	- **`sync` / `push` / `clone` / `validate-desc` /
241	  `fix-desc` / `finalize` / `symlink`** — still mostly
242	  inline `#[arg]` fields. The `--scope` retrofits queued in
243	  [`notes/todo.md`](notes/todo.md) are the usual entry
244	  point for converting one.
245	
246	## See also
247	
248	- [`README.md`](README.md) — user-facing overview and
249	  per-subcommand usage.
250	- [`CLAUDE.md`](CLAUDE.md) — bot workflow, versioning,
251	  commit/push conventions, code conventions.
252	- [`src/options_flags/README.md`](src/options_flags/README.md)
253	  — leaf/bundle patterns for Migration B.
254	- [`notes/todo.md`](notes/todo.md) — live task list,
255	  including both migrations.
256	- [`notes/chores-09.md`](notes/chores-09.md) — the
257	  0.43.0 / 0.44.0 design capture this document supersedes.
258

# vc-x1

- [Overview](#vc-x1)
- [Architecture](ARCHITECTURE.md)
- [Usage](#usage)
  - [Revision shortcuts](#revision-shortcuts)
  - [Shell completion](#shell-completion)
  - [validate-desc](#validate-desc)
  - [fix-desc](#fix-desc)
  - [clone](#clone)
  - [init](#init)
  - [symlink](#symlink)
  - [finalize](#finalize)
  - [Testing push + finalize](#testing-push--finalize)
- [Cross-repo Linking with Git Trailers](#cross-repo-linking-with-git-trailers)
- [jj Tips for Git Users](#jj-tips-for-git-users)
- [Thoughts for the future](#thoughts-for-the-future)
- [Contributing](#contributing)
- [License](#license)

This is experiment 1 to explore creating a Vibe Coding (vc) environment.
We will investigate ways of using the dual jj-git repo concept, explored
in [hw-jjg-bot](https://github.com/winksaville/hw-jjg-bot.git) to
initially make it easy to see how the code base evolved. This is
made possible by the fact that we have two repos one with the code
and one with the conversation with the bot.

I've chosen the jj-git environment because jj provides the concept that
each commit has an immutable changeID as well as the mutable commitID
of git. The idea is that each commit made on repo A writes the
changeID in the commit message to repo B. Thus there is a cross reference
between the two repos and this will allow vc-x1 to show how the repo
evolved and the entity (bot or human) can more clearly understand **how** and
most importantly **why** the code evolved.

The solution space is wide open, from trivial CLI, web or app based
(mobile/non-mobile). In addition, I could see this as an extension to
existing programming editors like vscode and zed or even creating our
own IDE for vc.

See [Initial commit with dual jj-git repos](./notes/chores-01.md#initial-commit-with-dual-jj-git-repos)
for how the initial commit was created with the dual jj-git repos. After
doing so and I then created this README.md file.

## Usage

```
vc-x1 list [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # List commits in a jj repo
vc-x1 desc [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # Show full description of a commit
vc-x1 chid [-r REVISION] [-n COMMITS]  # Print changeID(s) for a revision
```

> AGENT

I'm oriented. Here's the lay of the land:

**Project:** `vc-x1` — a Rust CLI (single binary, subcommands) for managing a **dual jj-git workspace**: an app repo + a `.claude` bot-session repo, cross-linked by `ochid:` trailers in commit messages. Currently at **v0.46.0**, branch `main`, working copy clean.

**Architecture (the active theme):** an in-progress refactor splitting each subcommand into two layers —
- **CLI layer** (clap-aware): `XxxArgs` `#[derive(Args)]` → builds `XxxParams`; shared flags live in `src/options_flags/`.
- **Subcommand layer** (clap-free): `fn x(ctx: &Context, params: &XxxParams) -> Result<…>`.
- Boundary: `impl From/TryFrom<&XxxArgs> for XxxParams`.

Two migrations tracked in `ARCHITECTURE.md` + `notes/todo.md`:
- **Migration A** (args → Context + Params): done for `init` (0.44.0) and `finalize` (0.46.0); remaining nine (`sync`, `clone`, `push`, `validate-desc`, `fix-desc`, `symlink`, plus `chid`/`desc`/`list`/`show` via the CommonArgs sweep) planned as a `0.47.0-N` multi-step cycle, one step per subcommand.
- **Migration B** (inline `#[arg]` → `src/options_flags/` leaves/bundles): `init` fully done; others mostly inline, `--scope` retrofits are the usual entry point.

**Recent commits:** 0.43.0 test-module extraction → 0.44.0 InitParams+Context → 0.45.0 ARCHITECTURE.md → 0.46.0 finalize migration.

**Workflow conventions I'll follow** (from CLAUDE.md): dual jj repos committed with `-R` last + `ochid:` trailers; every plan starts with a version bump (single-step vs multi-step `-N` ladder); per-file review checkpoints; `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` for the commit→push→finalize flow with two approval gates; hard stop after `finalize`; doc comments on every file/fn; `// OK:` on `unwrap*`.

What would you like to work on? The obvious next move per `notes/todo.md` #1 is kicking off the `0.47.0` subcommand-layer sweep (probably starting with `sync` or `symlink`), but I'll wait for your direction.

> DEVELOPER

1	//! The `finalize` subcommand: squash + set-bookmark + push a jj
2	//! repo, optionally detached so trailing writes land first.
3	//!
4	//! Built for the bot to atomically finalize its `.claude` session
5	//! repo at end-of-step. Every action is opt-in (`--squash`,
6	//! `--push`, `--detach`, `--delay`). The detached path re-execs
7	//! `vc-x1 finalize --exec …` so its output and errors survive the
8	//! parent exiting; failures are dropped as markers under
9	//! `~/.cache/vc-x1/finalize-status` and surfaced on the next
10	//! `vc-x1` run.
11	
12	use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
13	use std::process::Stdio;
14	use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};
15	
16	use clap::Args;
17	use log::{debug, info};
18	
19	use crate::common::run;
20	use crate::context::Context;
21	
22	/// Directory where the detached finalize child writes failure markers.
23	/// A subsequent `vc-x1` invocation scans this directory and surfaces
24	/// any failures to the user, closing the gap that the detached child's
25	/// non-zero exit isn't observable by the caller.
26	fn status_dir() -> Option<PathBuf> {
27	    std::env::var_os("HOME").map(|h| PathBuf::from(h).join(".cache/vc-x1/finalize-status"))
28	}
29	
30	/// Drop a failure marker for the detached child whose non-zero exit
31	/// the caller can't observe.
32	fn write_failure_marker(params: &FinalizeParams, err: &str) {
33	    let Some(dir) = status_dir() else {
34	        return;
35	    };
36	    if std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).is_err() {
37	        return;
38	    }
39	    let ns = SystemTime::now()
40	        .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
41	        .map(|d| d.as_nanos())
42	        .unwrap_or(0); // OK: filesystem-sortable timestamp; 0 on the impossible pre-epoch path
43	    let pid = std::process::id();
44	    let path = dir.join(format!("{ns}-{pid}.status"));
45	    let content = format!(
46	        "timestamp_ns={ns}\npid={pid}\nrepo={}\nbookmark={}\nerror={err}\n",
47	        params.repo.display(),
48	        params.bookmark.as_deref().unwrap_or(""),
49	    );
50	    let _ = std::fs::write(&path, content);
51	}
52	
53	/// Read, print, and delete any failure markers left by previous detached
54	/// finalize children. Cheap no-op when the directory doesn't exist.
55	pub fn surface_previous_failures() {
56	    let Some(dir) = status_dir() else {
57	        return;
58	    };
59	    let Ok(entries) = std::fs::read_dir(&dir) else {
60	        return;
61	    };
62	    let mut paths: Vec<PathBuf> = entries
63	        .flatten()
64	        .map(|e| e.path())
65	        .filter(|p| p.extension().and_then(|s| s.to_str()) == Some("status"))
66	        .collect();
67	    paths.sort();
68	    for path in paths {
69	        if let Ok(content) = std::fs::read_to_string(&path) {
70	            eprintln!(
71	                "warn: previous finalize failure ({}):",
72	                path.file_stem()
73	                    .map(|s| s.to_string_lossy())
74	                    .unwrap_or_default() // OK: filename without extension; empty on unexpected absence
75	            );
76	            for line in content.lines() {
77	                eprintln!("  {line}");
78	            }
79	            let _ = std::fs::remove_file(&path);
80	        }
81	    }
82	}
83	
84	/// Squash, set bookmark, and/or push a jj repo.
85	///
86	/// Designed for the bot to atomically finalize its session repo:
87	/// --detach exits immediately, --delay waits for trailing writes,
88	/// --squash folds them in, --bookmark + --push sends it upstream.
89	/// Every flag is opt-in. See README.md for details.
90	#[derive(Args, Debug)]
91	pub struct FinalizeArgs {
92	    /// Path to jj repo
93	    #[arg(long, default_value = ".")]
94	    pub repo: PathBuf,
95	
96	    /// Squash SOURCE into TARGET [default: @,@-]
97	    #[arg(long, value_name = "SOURCE,TARGET", default_missing_value = "@,@-", num_args = 0..=1)]
98	    pub squash: Option<String>,
99	
100	    /// Seconds to wait before squashing
101	    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 10.0)]
102	    pub delay: f64,
103	
104	    /// Existing bookmark to push to remote
105	    #[arg(long, value_name = "BOOKMARK")]
106	    pub push: Option<String>,
107	
108	    /// Detach and run in the background
109	    #[arg(long)]
110	    pub detach: bool,
111	
112	    /// Internal: run exec path (used by detach)
113	    #[arg(long, hide = true)]
114	    pub exec: bool,
115	}
116	
117	/// Parsed squash spec: source and target revisions.
118	#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
119	pub struct SquashSpec {
120	    pub source: String,
121	    pub target: String,
122	}
123	
124	impl SquashSpec {
125	    /// Parse a `SOURCE,TARGET` pair (e.g. `@,@-`); both halves
126	    /// must be non-empty.
127	    fn parse(s: &str) -> Result<Self, String> {
128	        let parts: Vec<&str> = s.split(',').collect();
129	        if parts.len() != 2 || parts[0].is_empty() || parts[1].is_empty() {
130	            return Err(format!(
131	                "invalid --squash value '{s}': expected SOURCE,TARGET (e.g. @,@-)"
132	            ));
133	        }
134	        Ok(SquashSpec {
135	            source: parts[0].to_string(),
136	            target: parts[1].to_string(),
137	        })
138	    }
139	}
140	
141	/// Per-invocation finalize inputs — the clap-free shape the
142	/// `finalize` op works against. Built from `FinalizeArgs` at the
143	/// binary edge via `TryFrom` (fallible: `--squash` parsing and
144	/// repo-path canonicalization). The `--log` path lives on
145	/// `Context`, not here.
146	#[derive(Debug)]
147	pub struct FinalizeParams {
148	    pub repo: PathBuf,
149	    pub squash: Option<SquashSpec>,
150	    pub delay_secs: f64,
151	    pub bookmark: Option<String>,
152	    pub push: bool,
153	    pub detach: bool,
154	    pub exec: bool,
155	}
156	
157	impl TryFrom<&FinalizeArgs> for FinalizeParams {
158	    type Error = String;
159	
160	    /// Parse `--squash`, derive `push`/`bookmark` from `--push`,
161	    /// and canonicalize `--repo` (so the detached child resolves it
162	    /// regardless of cwd).
163	    fn try_from(a: &FinalizeArgs) -> Result<Self, String> {
164	        let squash = a.squash.as_deref().map(SquashSpec::parse).transpose()?;
165	        let repo = std::fs::canonicalize(&a.repo)
166	            .map_err(|e| format!("cannot resolve repo path '{}': {e}", a.repo.display()))?;
167	        Ok(FinalizeParams {
168	            repo,
169	            squash,
170	            delay_secs: a.delay,
171	            bookmark: a.push.clone(),
172	            push: a.push.is_some(),
173	            detach: a.detach,
174	            exec: a.exec,
175	        })
176	    }
177	}
178	
179	/// Validate inputs synchronously before detaching.
180	///
181	/// Catches the common failure modes up front so the parent can exit
182	/// with a visible non-zero status — rather than discovering them in
183	/// the detached child where errors only reach the log file.
184	fn preflight(params: &FinalizeParams) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
185	    debug!("preflight: checking params");
186	    let repo_str = params.repo.to_string_lossy();
187	    let cwd = std::path::Path::new(".");
188	
189	    // Verify squash revsets resolve to something.
190	    if let Some(ref sq) = params.squash {
191	        jj_rev_exists(&repo_str, cwd, &sq.source)
192	            .map_err(|e| format!("squash source '{}' does not resolve: {e}", sq.source))?;
193	        jj_rev_exists(&repo_str, cwd, &sq.target)
194	            .map_err(|e| format!("squash target '{}' does not resolve: {e}", sq.target))?;
195	    }
196	
197	    // Refuse to operate on a repo with conflicts.
198	    let conflicts = run(
199	        "jj",
200	        &[
201	            "log",
202	            "-r",
203	            "conflicts()",
204	            "--no-graph",
205	            "-T",
206	            "\"x\"",
207	            "-R",
208	            &repo_str,
209	        ],
210	        cwd,
211	    )?;
212	    if !conflicts.is_empty() {
213	        return Err(format!("repo '{repo_str}' has conflicts — resolve before finalize").into());
214	    }
215	
216	    // Bookmark: existence, tracking, forward-only move, push-target description.
217	    if let Some(ref bookmark) = params.bookmark {
218	        let exists = run("jj", &["bookmark", "list", bookmark, "-R", &repo_str], cwd)?;
219	        if exists.is_empty() {
220	            return Err(format!("bookmark '{bookmark}' does not exist").into());
221	        }
222	
223	        crate::common::verify_tracking(&params.repo, bookmark)?;
224	
225	        let target_rev = params
226	            .squash
227	            .as_ref()
228	            .map(|sq| sq.target.as_str())
229	            .unwrap_or("@"); // OK: no squash spec → bookmark will point at current @
230	        let range = run(
231	            "jj",
232	            &[
233	                "log",
234	                "-r",
235	                &format!("{bookmark}::({target_rev})"),
236	                "--no-graph",
237	                "-T",
238	                "\"x\"",
239	                "-R",
240	                &repo_str,
241	            ],
242	            cwd,
243	        )?;
244	        if range.is_empty() {
245	            return Err(format!(
246	                "bookmark '{bookmark}' move is not forward — current position is not an \
247	                 ancestor of '{target_rev}' (would diverge)"
248	            )
249	            .into());
250	        }
251	
252	        if params.push {
253	            let desc = run(
254	                "jj",
255	                &[
256	                    "log",
257	                    "-r",
258	                    target_rev,
259	                    "--no-graph",
260	                    "-T",
261	                    "description",
262	                    "-R",
263	                    &repo_str,
264	                ],
265	                cwd,
266	            )?;
267	            if desc.trim().is_empty() {
268	                return Err(format!(
269	                    "push target '{target_rev}' has no description — push would fail \
270	                     (run `jj describe -r {target_rev} -R {repo_str}` first)"
271	                )
272	                .into());
273	            }
274	        }
275	    }
276	
277	    log_plan(params)?;
278	
279	    Ok(())
280	}
281	
282	/// Return Ok(()) if the revset resolves to one or more commits in `repo`.
283	fn jj_rev_exists(repo: &str, cwd: &std::path::Path, rev: &str) -> Result<(), String> {
284	    run(
285	        "jj",
286	        &["log", "-r", rev, "--no-graph", "-T", "\"x\"", "-R", repo],
287	        cwd,
288	    )
289	    .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
290	    Ok(())
291	}
292	
293	/// Log what finalize is about to do so the user sees the plan before detach.
294	fn log_plan(params: &FinalizeParams) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
295	    let repo_str = params.repo.to_string_lossy();
296	    let cwd = std::path::Path::new(".");
297	
298	    if let Some(ref sq) = params.squash {
299	        info!(
300	            "finalize: squash {} → {} in {}",
301	            sq.source, sq.target, repo_str
302	        );
303	    }
304	    if let Some(ref bookmark) = params.bookmark {
305	        let target_rev = params
306	            .squash
307	            .as_ref()
308	            .map(|sq| sq.target.as_str())
309	            .unwrap_or("@"); // OK: same as above
310	        let current = jj_rev_short(&repo_str, cwd, bookmark).unwrap_or_else(|_| "?".into()); // OK: logging only — fall back to "?" if revset fails
311	        let target = jj_rev_short(&repo_str, cwd, target_rev).unwrap_or_else(|_| "?".into()); // OK: same
312	        info!("finalize: set bookmark '{bookmark}' {current} → {target} ({target_rev})");
313	        if params.push {
314	            info!("finalize: push '{bookmark}' to remote");
315	        }
316	    }
317	
318	    Ok(())
319	}
320	
321	/// Short one-line summary of a revset: `<change_short> <commit_short>`.
322	fn jj_rev_short(repo: &str, cwd: &std::path::Path, rev: &str) -> Result<String, String> {
323	    run(
324	        "jj",
325	        &[
326	            "log",
327	            "-r",
328	            rev,
329	            "--no-graph",
330	            "-T",
331	            "change_id.shortest(8) ++ \" \" ++ commit_id.shortest(8)",
332	            "-R",
333	            repo,
334	        ],
335	        cwd,
336	    )
337	    .map_err(|e| e.to_string())
338	}
339	
340	/// Do the squash / set-bookmark / push work — runs in the detached
341	/// child when `--detach`, inline otherwise.
342	fn finalize_exec(params: &FinalizeParams) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
343	    debug!("finalize_exec: starting params={params:?}");
344	    let repo_str = params.repo.to_string_lossy();
345	    let cwd = std::path::Path::new(".");
346	
347	    // Squash if requested
348	    if let Some(ref sq) = params.squash {
349	        let delay = std::time::Duration::from_secs_f64(params.delay_secs);
350	        debug!("finalize_exec: sleeping {delay:?}");
351	        std::thread::sleep(delay);
352	
353	        info!("finalize: squashing {} → {}...", sq.source, sq.target);
354	        run(
355	            "jj",
356	            &[
357	                "squash",
358	                "--ignore-immutable",
359	                "--use-destination-message",
360	                "--from",
361	                &sq.source,
362	                "--into",
363	                &sq.target,
364	                "-R",
365	                &repo_str,
366	            ],
367	            cwd,
368	        )?;
369	    }
370	
371	    // Set bookmark and push if requested
372	    if let Some(ref bookmark) = params.bookmark {
373	        // Verify bookmark exists — don't silently create new ones
374	        let result = run("jj", &["bookmark", "list", bookmark, "-R", &repo_str], cwd)?;
375	        if result.is_empty() {
376	            return Err(format!("bookmark '{bookmark}' does not exist").into());
377	        }
378	
379	        let rev = params
380	            .squash
381	            .as_ref()
382	            .map(|sq| sq.target.as_str())
383	            .unwrap_or("@"); // OK: no squash spec → bookmark points at current @
384	        info!("finalize: setting bookmark '{bookmark}' to {rev}...");
385	        run(
386	            "jj",
387	            &["bookmark", "set", bookmark, "-r", rev, "-R", &repo_str],
388	            cwd,
389	        )?;
390	
391	        if params.push {
392	            info!("finalize: pushing '{bookmark}' to origin...");
393	            run(
394	                "jj",
395	                &["git", "push", "--bookmark", bookmark, "-R", &repo_str],
396	                &params.repo,
397	            )?;
398	        }
399	    }
400	
401	    info!("finalize: done");
402	    debug!("finalize_exec: done");
403	    Ok(())
404	}
405	
406	/// Build the argv for the detached re-exec: `finalize --exec …`,
407	/// mirroring `params` plus the `--log` path so the child logs to
408	/// the same file.
409	pub fn build_exec_args(params: &FinalizeParams, log: Option<&Path>) -> Vec<String> {
410	    let mut args = vec![
411	        "finalize".to_string(),
412	        "--exec".to_string(),
413	        "--repo".to_string(),
414	        params.repo.to_string_lossy().to_string(),
415	    ];
416	    if let Some(ref sq) = params.squash {
417	        args.push("--squash".to_string());
418	        args.push(format!("{},{}", sq.source, sq.target));
419	        args.push("--delay".to_string());
420	        args.push(params.delay_secs.to_string());
421	    }
422	    if let Some(ref bookmark) = params.bookmark {
423	        args.push("--push".to_string());
424	        args.push(bookmark.clone());
425	    }
426	    if let Some(log) = log {
427	        args.push("--log".to_string());
428	        args.push(log.to_string_lossy().to_string());
429	    }
430	    args
431	}
432	
433	/// Spawn a detached child process with `--exec` and return immediately.
434	/// The child re-enters `main()`, parses `--exec`, and `finalize()` routes
435	/// it to `finalize_exec()` where the actual work happens.
436	fn detach(ctx: &Context, params: &FinalizeParams) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
437	    debug!("detach: parent starting params={params:?}");
438	
439	    let exe = std::env::current_exe()?;
440	    let args = build_exec_args(params, ctx.log.as_deref());
441	    debug!("detach: exe={exe:?} args={args:?}");
442	
443	    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new(exe);
444	    cmd.args(&args).stdin(Stdio::null());
445	
446	    // Hand the child the user's controlling terminal when one exists so
447	    // its output stays visible after the parent exits. Inherited pipes
448	    // are unreliable here — the bash-tool / cron caller typically closes
449	    // its read end on parent exit, silently dropping child writes. Fall
450	    // back to null when there is no tty (pipe-invoked / CI / Windows);
451	    // the log file is authoritative in that case.
452	    match std::fs::OpenOptions::new()
453	        .read(true)
454	        .write(true)
455	        .open("/dev/tty")
456	    {
457	        Ok(tty) => {
458	            debug!("detach: /dev/tty opened, child output → terminal");
459	            let out = tty.try_clone()?;
460	            cmd.stdout(Stdio::from(out));
461	            cmd.stderr(Stdio::from(tty));
462	        }
463	        Err(e) => {
464	            debug!("detach: /dev/tty unavailable ({e}), child output → null");
465	            cmd.stdout(Stdio::null());
466	            cmd.stderr(Stdio::null());
467	        }
468	    }
469	
470	    #[cfg(unix)]
471	    {
472	        use std::os::unix::process::CommandExt;
473	        unsafe {
474	            cmd.pre_exec(|| {
475	                libc::setsid();
476	                Ok(())
477	            });
478	        }
479	    }
480	
481	    let child = cmd.spawn()?;
482	    debug!("detach: spawned child pid={}", child.id());
483	    match &ctx.log {
484	        Some(log) => info!(
485	            "finalize: detached (pid {}), log: {}",
486	            child.id(),
487	            log.display()
488	        ),
489	        None => info!("finalize: detached (pid {})", child.id()),
490	    }
491	    Ok(())
492	}
493	
494	/// Run the `finalize` subcommand: preflight, then either detach a
495	/// child to do the work or do it inline.
496	pub fn finalize(ctx: &Context, params: &FinalizeParams) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
497	    // Nothing to do — show help hint
498	    if params.squash.is_none() && !params.push {
499	        return Err("nothing to do (see --help for options)".into());
500	    }
501	
502	    debug!("finalize: entry params={params:?}");
503	
504	    // Validate synchronously before detaching so failures exit with a
505	    // visible non-zero status rather than hiding in the detached child's log.
506	    // Skip in --exec (the re-entered child already passed preflight in the parent).
507	    if !params.exec {
508	        preflight(params)?;
509	    }
510	
511	    let result = if params.detach && !params.exec {
512	        detach(ctx, params)
513	    } else {
514	        finalize_exec(params)
515	    };
516	    match &result {
517	        Ok(()) => debug!("finalize: exit ok"),
518	        Err(e) => {
519	            debug!("finalize: exit err={e}");
520	            // In the detached-child path the caller can't observe our
521	            // non-zero exit. Drop a failure marker so a later vc-x1
522	            // invocation can surface the problem.
523	            if params.exec {
524	                write_failure_marker(params, &e.to_string());
525	            }
526	        }
527	    }
528	    result
529	}

539	    fn parse(args: &[&str]) -> FinalizeArgs {
540	        let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(args).unwrap();
541	        match cli.command {

553	        let args = parse(&["vc-x1", "finalize"]);
554	        assert_eq!(args.repo, PathBuf::from("."));

556	        assert!(args.push.is_none());

563	        assert!(args.squash.is_none());

565	    }
566	
567	    #[test]

573	            "finalize",

576	            "--squash",

584	        .unwrap();
585	        assert_eq!(cli.log, Some(PathBuf::from("/tmp/test.log")));
586	        if let Commands::Finalize(args) = cli.command {

589	            assert_eq!(args.push, Some("dev-0.14.0".to_string()));
590	            assert_eq!(args.delay, 2.5);

670	            assert_eq!(parsed.squash.as_ref().unwrap().source, "@");

673	            assert_eq!(parsed.delay_secs, params.delay_secs);
674	            assert!(parsed.push);

22	/// Canonical shape for a Flag (boolean-domain) leaf's typed
23	/// value-parser. Conditional contract — implemented only when a
24	/// boolean leaf takes a value form (e.g. `--flag=true|false`).
25	/// Presence/absence flags don't need an impl; clap parses
26	/// directly. See [Marker traits](README.md#marker-traits).
27	#[allow(
28	    dead_code,

30	)]
31	pub trait FlagParser {
32	    /// Typed value the parser produces (typically `bool`).
33	    type Value;
34	
35	    /// Parse a CLI string into the typed value.

37	}

42	/// leaves need no impl. See

45	    dead_code,

50	    type Value;
51	
52	    /// Parse a CLI string into the typed value.

1	# options_flags — Reusable CLI Options and Flags
2	
3	Per-flag CLI surfaces shared across multiple subcommands. Each
4	shared option or flag (OF) lives in its own submodule so its
5	type, parser, and tests stay together.
6	
7	This module is the CLI-layer leaf store for "Migration B" in
8	[`../../ARCHITECTURE.md`](../../ARCHITECTURE.md); see there for
9	how the leaves fit the wider clap-args / `Context`+`Params`
10	split.
11	
12	## Architecture
13	
14	Three composition patterns:
15	
16	- **Leaf** — a `#[derive(Args)]` struct holding the flag(s),
17	  `value_parser`, default, and help text (via the field
18	  doc-comment). One `#[derive(Args)]` per logical OF — single
19	  flag or a small fixed pair (e.g. `push_retry`'s two fields).
20	  Help text aims to be generic enough for any reasonable
21	  consumer.
22	- **Bundle** — a `#[derive(Args)]` that flattens N leaves into a
23	  named role (e.g. `ProvisionOptionFlagBundle`). One
24	  `#[command(flatten)]` line at the consumer picks up the whole
25	  bundle.
26	- **Pattern A escape hatch** — when a consumer needs unique help
27	  text (or different defaults), it skips the leaf's flatten and
28	  inlines its own `#[arg(value_parser = …)]` field, reusing the
29	  leaf's typed value and parser.
30	
31	## Flag vs Option — classify by domain, not wire syntax
32	
33	Every leaf falls into one of two categories, picked by the value
34	domain it carries:
35	
36	- **Flag** — boolean domain. Wire form may be presence/absence
37	  (`--dry-run`), explicit negation (`--no-dry-run`), or value
38	  (`--dry-run=true|false`). All three express a `bool`. Type
39	  name suffix: `*Flag`.
40	- **Option** — non-boolean domain (string, integer, enum, path,
41	  …). Wire form is always `--name=<value>` (or `--name <value>`).
42	  Type name suffix: `*Option` (or `*Options` for a multi-field
43	  leaf like `PushRetryOptions`).
44	
45	Wire form is presentation; the category is the underlying domain.
46	
47	## Adding a new leaf
48	
49	1. Create `options_flags/<name>.rs` with a
50	   `#[derive(Args, Debug, Clone, Default)]` struct.
51	2. Pick the suffix per domain: `*Flag` (boolean) or `*Option(s)`
52	   (non-boolean).
53	3. Add `pub mod <name>;` to `mod.rs`.
54	4. If the leaf has explicit parsing logic, declare a unit-struct
55	   implementor of `FlagParser` (boolean domain) or `OptionParser`
56	   (non-boolean domain). Bare `Option<String>` leaves and
57	   presence/absence boolean leaves need no parser impl — clap
58	   handles them.
59	5. Add tests for any non-trivial parser/resolver logic.
60	6. Leaves do **not** implement a Bundle marker — bundles do.
61	
62	## Consuming an OF
63	
64	Default — flatten the leaf into your subcommand's `Args`:
65	
66	```rust
67	#[derive(Args)]
68	pub struct MyArgs {
69	    #[command(flatten)]
70	    pub config: ConfigOption,
71	    // ...
72	}
73	```
74	
75	Pattern A — when generic help doesn't fit:
76	
77	```rust
78	#[derive(Args)]
79	pub struct MyArgs {
80	    /// My subcommand-specific help for --config.
81	    #[arg(long = "config", value_name = "none|PATH",
82	          verbatim_doc_comment)]
83	    pub config: Option<String>,
84	    // ...
85	}
86	```
87	
88	A Pattern A consumer reuses the leaf's types (e.g. `ConfigKind`)
89	and parsers (e.g. `parse_config_kind`) but owns its own clap
90	attributes.
91	
92	## Consumer function shape
93	
94	Helper functions called by a subcommand body should accept the
95	relevant leaf type by reference rather than unpacking individual
96	fields at the call site:
97	
98	```rust
99	// Multi-field leaf — pass the whole leaf
100	fn run_retry(cmd: &str, args: &[&str], cwd: &Path,
101	             retry: &PushRetryOptions) -> Result<…> { … }
102	
103	run_retry("git", &["push", …], cwd, &args.provision.push_retry)?;
104	```
105	
106	This wins on:
107	- Readability — `&args.provision.push_retry` reads as a single
108	  unit; the function body works with `retry.push_retries` (no
109	  leaf-name doubling because the parameter name is the consumer's
110	  choice).
111	- Argument count — every multi-field leaf collapses N args into
112	  one ref, so chained helpers don't accumulate
113	  `clippy::too_many_arguments` warnings.
114	- Future-proofing — extending a leaf with another field is a
115	  zero-touch change at every call site.
116	
117	For **single-field leaves** (e.g. `DryRunFlag`, `PrivateFlag`),
118	direct read at the consumer site (`args.provision.dry_run.dry_run`)
119	is fine — wrapping a `bool` in `&LeafType` parameter doesn't earn
120	the indirection.
121	
122	## Marker traits
123	
124	Three Bundle markers — one per content classification. Bundles
125	implement exactly one. Leaves do **not** implement these markers
126	(their category lives in the type-name suffix).
127	
128	- `FlagBundle: clap::Args` — pure-boolean bundle (every
129	  constituent leaf is a Flag). Rare in practice.
130	- `OptionBundle: clap::Args` — pure-non-boolean bundle (every
131	  constituent leaf is an Option).
132	- `OptionFlagBundle: clap::Args` — mixed bundle (constituents
133	  include both Flag and Option leaves). Most common in practice.
134	
135	Two Parser traits — conditional contracts implemented only when
136	a leaf has explicit parsing logic (custom `value_parser`):
137	
138	- `FlagParser` — boolean-domain parser. Implemented only for
139	  boolean leaves that take a value form (`--flag=true|false`).
140	  Presence/absence flags need no impl; clap parses directly.
141	- `OptionParser` — non-boolean-domain parser. Implemented only
142	  for leaves with custom parsing logic (e.g. `ScopeParser`,
143	  `RepoParser`); bare `Option<String>` leaves need no impl.
144	
145	`FlagParser` and `OptionParser` share an identical method shape;
146	the difference is documentation (which value-domain the parser
147	targets).
148	
149	All five traits are documentation-level — clap derive doesn't
150	see them; the discipline is that every leaf and bundle declares
151	the appropriate ones explicitly.
152	
153	## Bundle marker discipline
154	
155	Bundle markers aren't compiler-checked against contents. If a
156	bundle's leaf set shifts category — e.g. a pure-Flag bundle gains
157	an Option leaf — you must update the marker by hand
158	(`FlagBundle` → `OptionFlagBundle`). No lint, no failure; silent
159	drift if forgotten. Worth a glance at the marker line whenever a
160	bundle's `#[command(flatten)]` set changes.
161	
162	## Pattern A worked example
163	
164	`init`'s `--config` field originally carried init-specific help
165	("Only valid with `--scope=por`..."). It currently flattens
166	`ConfigOption` for the generic help; the `--scope=por` constraint
167	surfaces in `init`'s preflight error. If the help-text generality
168	becomes a usability problem, init will switch to Pattern A as the
169	worked example.
170	
171	## Layout note
172	
173	OFs currently sit as flat `<name>.rs` files alongside this
174	README. Bundle modules carry the `_bundle` suffix
175	(e.g. `provision_bundle.rs`) so the leaf/bundle split is visible
176	in the directory listing without opening files. If an individual
177	OF accumulates enough rationale, edge-case detail, or examples
178	to outgrow doc-comments, it will graduate to a `<name>/mod.rs` +
179	`<name>/README.md` subdirectory layout. Mechanical when needed;
180	not done preemptively.
181	
```

1	//! `--scope` — code,bot|por target set selector.
2	//! See [options_flags](README.md) for shared architecture.
3	
4	use clap::Args;
5	
6	/// Typed value of `--scope` — the kinds of repo set a
7	/// subcommand can target.
8	///
9	/// - `CodeBot` (default) — dual-repo: code + `.claude` bot
10	///   session.
11	/// - `Por` — single repo (Plain Old Repo); no `.claude/`,
12	///   no `.vc-config.toml`.
13	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Default)]
14	pub enum ScopeKind {
15	    #[default]
16	    CodeBot,
17	    Por,
18	}
19	
20	/// Parse the `--scope` value into a `ScopeKind`.
21	///
22	/// - Accepts `code,bot` / `bot,code` (commutative) and `por`.
23	/// - Standalone `code` or `bot` errors — these are
24	///   config-lookup keywords; subcommands using `ScopeKind`
25	///   have no config-driven sides to look up against.
26	pub fn parse_scope_kind(s: &str) -> Result<ScopeKind, String> {
27	    match s {
28	        "code,bot" | "bot,code" => Ok(ScopeKind::CodeBot),
29	        "por" => Ok(ScopeKind::Por),
30	        "code" | "bot" => Err(format!(
31	            "'--scope={s}' is not a valid scope kind — use 'code,bot' (dual) or 'por' (single)"
32	        )),
33	        _ => Err(format!(
34	            "'--scope={s}' is not recognized — expected 'code,bot' or 'por'"
35	        )),
36	    }
37	}
38	
39	/// `OptionParser` impl for `--scope` (non-boolean domain).
40	/// Documentation-level — consumers can use either
41	/// `parse_scope_kind` directly or `ScopeParser::parse`.
42	pub struct ScopeParser;
43	
44	impl super::OptionParser for ScopeParser {
45	    type Value = ScopeKind;
46	
47	    fn parse(s: &str) -> Result<Self::Value, String> {
48	        parse_scope_kind(s)
49	    }
50	}
51	
52	/// `--scope` leaf (Option — non-boolean domain) — see
53	/// [Consuming an OF](README.md#consuming-an-of).
54	#[derive(Args, Debug, Clone, Default)]
55	pub struct ScopeOption {
56	    /// Scope — `code,bot` (dual, default) or `por` (single).
57	    #[arg(
58	        long,
59	        short,
60	        value_name = "SCOPE",
61	        value_parser = parse_scope_kind,
62	        default_value = "code,bot",
63	        verbatim_doc_comment
64	    )]
65	    pub scope: ScopeKind,
66	}
67	
68	#[cfg(test)]
69	mod tests {
70	    use super::*;
71	
72	    #[test]
73	    fn scope_kind_code_bot() {
74	        assert_eq!(parse_scope_kind("code,bot").unwrap(), ScopeKind::CodeBot);
75	    }
76	
77	    #[test]
78	    fn scope_kind_bot_code_commutative() {
79	        assert_eq!(parse_scope_kind("bot,code").unwrap(), ScopeKind::CodeBot);
80	    }
81	
82	    #[test]
83	    fn scope_kind_por() {
84	        assert_eq!(parse_scope_kind("por").unwrap(), ScopeKind::Por);
85	    }
86	
87	    #[test]
88	    fn scope_kind_code_alone_errors() {
89	        let err = parse_scope_kind("code").unwrap_err();
90	        assert!(err.contains("not a valid scope kind"), "got: {err}");
91	    }
92	
93	    #[test]
94	    fn scope_kind_bot_alone_errors() {
95	        let err = parse_scope_kind("bot").unwrap_err();
96	        assert!(err.contains("not a valid scope kind"), "got: {err}");
97	    }
98	
99	    #[test]
100	    fn scope_kind_unknown_errors() {
101	        let err = parse_scope_kind("xyz").unwrap_err();
102	        assert!(err.contains("not recognized"), "got: {err}");
103	    }
104	}

1	//! `--repo` — pick a repo target via the user-config
2	//! account chain. See [options_flags](README.md) for shared
3	//! architecture.
4	
5	use clap::Args;
6	
7	use crate::config::RepoSelector;
8	
9	/// Parse the `--repo` value into a `config::RepoSelector`.
10	///
11	/// - `<cat>` → `RepoSelector { category, value: None }`.
12	/// - `<cat>=<val>` → `RepoSelector { category, value: Some(val) }`.
13	/// - Empty input, empty category, or empty value (after `=`)
14	///   errors.
15	pub fn parse_repo_arg(s: &str) -> Result<RepoSelector, String> {
16	    let s = s.trim();
17	    if s.is_empty() {
18	        return Err("--repo: value is empty".into());
19	    }
20	    match s.split_once('=') {
21	        Some((cat, val)) => {
22	            let cat = cat.trim();
23	            let val = val.trim();
24	            if cat.is_empty() {
25	                return Err(format!("--repo: missing category in '{s}'"));
26	            }
27	            if val.is_empty() {
28	                return Err(format!("--repo: empty value in '{s}'"));
29	            }
30	            Ok(RepoSelector {
31	                category: cat.to_string(),
32	                value: Some(val.to_string()),
33	            })
34	        }
35	        None => Ok(RepoSelector {
36	            category: s.to_string(),
37	            value: None,
38	        }),
39	    }
40	}
41	
42	/// `OptionParser` impl for `--repo` (non-boolean domain).
43	/// Documentation-level — consumers can use either
44	/// `parse_repo_arg` directly or `RepoParser::parse`.
45	pub struct RepoParser;
46	
47	impl super::OptionParser for RepoParser {
48	    type Value = RepoSelector;
49	
50	    fn parse(s: &str) -> Result<Self::Value, String> {
51	        parse_repo_arg(s)
52	    }
53	}
54	
55	/// `--repo` leaf (Option — non-boolean domain) — see
56	/// [Consuming an OF](README.md#consuming-an-of).
57	#[derive(Args, Debug, Clone, Default)]
58	pub struct RepoOption {
59	    /// Repo target — `<cat>` or `<cat>=<val>`.
60	    ///
61	    /// - `<cat>` looks up the value via the account chain
62	    ///   in the user config.
63	    /// - `<cat>=<val>` uses the literal value, no config
64	    ///   lookup needed.
65	    /// - Specific category meanings (`remote`, `local`,
66	    ///   …) depend on the consumer subcommand.
67	    #[arg(
68	        long,
69	        value_name = "CAT[=VAL]",
70	        value_parser = parse_repo_arg,
71	        verbatim_doc_comment
72	    )]
73	    pub repo: Option<RepoSelector>,
74	}
75	
76	#[cfg(test)]
77	mod tests {
78	    use super::*;
79	
80	    #[test]
81	    fn repo_arg_category_only() {
82	        let sel = parse_repo_arg("remote").unwrap();
83	        assert_eq!(sel.category, "remote");
84	        assert_eq!(sel.value, None);
85	    }
86	
87	    #[test]
88	    fn repo_arg_category_and_value() {
89	        let sel = parse_repo_arg("local=/tmp/fixtures").unwrap();
90	        assert_eq!(sel.category, "local");
91	        assert_eq!(sel.value.as_deref(), Some("/tmp/fixtures"));
92	    }
93	
94	    #[test]
95	    fn repo_arg_trims_whitespace() {
96	        let sel = parse_repo_arg("  remote = git@github.com:foo  ").unwrap();
97	        assert_eq!(sel.category, "remote");
98	        assert_eq!(sel.value.as_deref(), Some("git@github.com:foo"));
99	    }
100	
101	    #[test]
102	    fn repo_arg_empty_errors() {
103	        let err = parse_repo_arg("").unwrap_err();
104	        assert!(err.contains("empty"), "got: {err}");
105	    }
106	
107	    #[test]
108	    fn repo_arg_missing_category_errors() {
109	        let err = parse_repo_arg("=foo").unwrap_err();
110	        assert!(err.contains("missing category"), "got: {err}");
111	    }
112	
113	    #[test]
114	    fn repo_arg_empty_value_errors() {
115	        let err = parse_repo_arg("remote=").unwrap_err();
116	        assert!(err.contains("empty value"), "got: {err}");
117	    }
118	}
119

1	//! `--push-retries` + `--push-retry-delay` — retry policy for
2	//! the post-create `git push` (waits out remote provisioner
3	//! propagation lag, e.g. GitHub's async repo creation).
4	//! See [options_flags](README.md) for shared architecture.
5	
6	use clap::Args;
7	
8	/// `--push-retries` / `--push-retry-delay` leaf (Options — both
9	/// fields are non-boolean domain) — see
10	/// [Consuming an OF](README.md#consuming-an-of).
11	#[derive(Args, Debug, Clone)]
12	pub struct PushRetryOptions {
13	    /// Max push retries after repo creation [default: 5]
14	    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 5)]
15	    pub push_retries: u32,
16	
17	    /// Seconds between push retries [default: 3]
18	    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 3)]
19	    pub push_retry_delay: u64,
20	}
21	
22	impl Default for PushRetryOptions {
23	    /// Mirrors the clap defaults so fixture code can use
24	    /// `PushRetryOptions::default()` and stay aligned with what
25	    /// clap produces when the flags are absent.
26	    fn default() -> Self {
27	        Self {
28	            push_retries: 5,
29	            push_retry_delay: 3,
30	        }
31	    }
32	}

301	        Err(e) => {
302	            e.exit();

306	    let log_path = cli.log.as_ref().map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().to_string());
307	    logging::CliLogger::init(cli.verbose, log_path.as_deref());
308	
309	    // The detached `finalize --exec` re-entry is the bot's
310	    // session-end child; it shouldn't print the banner (extra
311	    // chatter in its log) or surface user-facing failure markers
312	    // (those are meant for the user's next interactive run).
313	    let is_detached_exec = matches!(
314	        cli.command,
315	        Commands::Finalize(ref f) if f.exec
316	    );
317	
318	    // `-L` / `--no-label` on the read-only multi-repo subcommands
319	    // (chid, desc, list, show) makes their output script-parseable
320	    // — adding a leading `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` line would break that. Skip
321	    // the banner whenever the active subcommand has the flag set.
322	    let suppress_banner = match &cli.command {
323	        Commands::Chid(a) => a.common.no_label,
324	        Commands::Desc(a) => a.common.no_label,
325	        Commands::List(a) => a.common.no_label,
326	        Commands::Show(a) => a.common.no_label,
327	        _ => false,
328	    };
329	
330	    if !is_detached_exec {
331	        if !suppress_banner {
332	            // Banner on every normal run, mirroring what `--help`
333	            // shows at the top.
334	            log::info!("{BANNER}");
335	        }
336	        finalize::surface_previous_failures();
337	    }
338	
339	    // Command name for bm-track output (first positional arg after
340	    // the binary; clap has already validated it by the time we get here).
341	    let command_name = std::env::args().nth(1).unwrap_or_else(|| "?".to_string()); // OK: default when somehow invoked without a subcommand
342	
343	    if !is_detached_exec {
344	        bm_track("enter", &command_name);
345	    }
346	
347	    let exit_code = match cli.command {
348	        Commands::Chid(chid_args) => run_command(chid::chid(&chid_args)),
349	        Commands::Desc(desc_args) => run_command(desc::desc(&desc_args)),
350	        Commands::List(list_args) => run_command(list::list(&list_args)),
351	        Commands::Show(show_args) => run_command(show::show(&show_args)),
352	        Commands::ValidateDesc(validate_desc_args) => {
353	            run_command(validate_desc::validate_desc(&validate_desc_args))
354	        }
355	        Commands::FixDesc(fix_desc_args) => run_command(fix_desc::fix_desc(&fix_desc_args)),
356	        Commands::Clone(clone_args) => run_command(clone::clone_repo(&clone_args)),
357	        Commands::Init(init_args) => {
358	            let ctx = match context::Context::load(cli.log) {
359	                Ok(c) => c,
360	                Err(e) => {
361	                    error!("{e}");
362	                    return ExitCode::FAILURE;
363	                }
364	            };
365	            let params = init::InitParams::from(&init_args);
366	            run_command(init::init(&ctx, &params))
367	        }
368	        Commands::Symlink(symlink_args) => run_command(symlink::symlink(&symlink_args)),
369	        Commands::Sync(sync_args) => run_command(sync::sync(&sync_args)),
370	        Commands::Finalize(finalize_args) => {
371	            let ctx = match context::Context::load(cli.log) {
372	                Ok(c) => c,
373	                Err(e) => {
374	                    error!("{e}");
375	                    return ExitCode::FAILURE;
376	                }
377	            };
378	            let params = match finalize::FinalizeParams::try_from(&finalize_args) {
379	                Ok(p) => p,
380	                Err(e) => {
381	                    error!("{e}");
382	                    return ExitCode::FAILURE;
383	                }
384	            };
385	            run_command(finalize::finalize(&ctx, &params))
386	        }
387	        Commands::Push(push_args) => run_command(push::push(&push_args)),
388	    };
389	
390	    if !is_detached_exec {
391	        bm_track("exit ", &command_name);
392	    }
393	
394	    exit_code
```

> AGENT

Here's what "Migration B for `finalize`" entails, based on `notes/todo.md` and the `options_flags` conventions:

## Plan

**Scope:** Only `--squash` has a (planned) second consumer (`vc-x1 push --squash`), so it's the one flag to lift. `--delay` / `--detach` / `--exec` / `--repo` / `--push` stay inline by design ("until a second consumer surfaces") — that's the documented terminal state, not unfinished work.

**Version:** single-step, **0.47.0**, `refactor:` type, one commit. Note: `notes/todo.md` currently earmarks `0.47.0-N` for the Migration A subcommand sweep — I'd shift that earmark to `0.48.0-N` as part of this change. (Flag if you'd rather this be `0.46.1` or want a different number.)

**Changes:**

1. **New `src/options_flags/squash.rs`** — move `SquashSpec` + `SquashSpec::parse` here (made `pub`), add a `SquashSpecParser` unit-struct `OptionParser` impl (per leaf conventions), add `PartialEq, Eq` to `SquashSpec`, and define the `SquashOption` leaf:
   ```rust
   #[derive(Args, Debug, Clone, Default)]
   pub struct SquashOption {
       /// Squash SOURCE into TARGET [default: @,@-]
       #[arg(long, value_name = "SOURCE,TARGET", value_parser = SquashSpec::parse,
             default_missing_value = "@,@-", num_args = 0..=1)]
       pub squash: Option<SquashSpec>,
   }
   ```
   This makes `--squash @` error at parse time (it currently errors later in `TryFrom`) — net UX improvement, matches how `scope`/`repo` leaves use `value_parser`.

2. **`src/options_flags/mod.rs`** — add `pub mod squash;`.

3. **`src/finalize.rs`** — drop the local `SquashSpec` + its tests `squash_parse_valid/invalid` (move them to `squash.rs`); replace `FinalizeArgs.squash: Option<String>` with `#[command(flatten)] pub squash: SquashOption`; simplify `FinalizeParams::try_from` (`squash` is already parsed); update imports and the in-file tests' `args.squash` → `args.squash.squash`.

4. **Docs:** `ARCHITECTURE.md` (add `squash` to the leaf list; update the Migration B "State today" entry for `finalize`); `notes/chores-09.md` (new `## finalize Migration B — --squash leaf (0.47.0)` subsection); `notes/todo.md` (move the Migration B item to `## Done` with a chores ref; shift the Migration A cycle earmark to `0.48.0-N`). No change to `README.md` / `notes/README.md` (no user-facing surface change).

I'll do this file-by-file with a review pause after each per CLAUDE.md. Sound good — and is **0.47.0** the right version, or do you want it slotted differently?

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/options_flags/squash.rs",
  "content": "//! `--squash SOURCE,TARGET` — pick the `jj squash --from/--into`\n//! revision pair (e.g. `@,@-`). See [options_flags](README.md) for\n//! shared architecture.\n\nuse clap::Args;\n\n/// Parsed `--squash` value: the source and target revisions.\n///\n/// - Produced by [`SquashSpec::parse`] (also wired as the leaf's\n///   `value_parser`, so clap rejects malformed input at parse time).\n/// - Both halves are guaranteed non-empty.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct SquashSpec {\n    pub source: String,\n    pub target: String,\n}\n\nimpl SquashSpec {\n    /// Parse a `SOURCE,TARGET` pair (e.g. `@,@-`); both halves\n    /// must be non-empty.\n    pub fn parse(s: &str) -> Result<Self, String> {\n        let parts: Vec<&str> = s.split(',').collect();\n        if parts.len() != 2 || parts[0].is_empty() || parts[1].is_empty() {\n            return Err(format!(\n                \"invalid --squash value '{s}': expected SOURCE,TARGET (e.g. @,@-)\"\n            ));\n        }\n        Ok(SquashSpec {\n            source: parts[0].to_string(),\n            target: parts[1].to_string(),\n        })\n    }\n}\n\n/// `OptionParser` impl for `--squash` (non-boolean domain).\n/// Documentation-level — consumers can use either\n/// `SquashSpec::parse` directly or `SquashSpecParser::parse`.\npub struct SquashSpecParser;\n\nimpl super::OptionParser for SquashSpecParser {\n    type Value = SquashSpec;\n\n    fn parse(s: &str) -> Result<Self::Value, String> {\n        SquashSpec::parse(s)\n    }\n}\n\n/// `--squash` leaf (Option — non-boolean domain) — see\n/// [Consuming an OF](README.md#consuming-an-of).\n///\n/// - Bare `--squash` (no value) defaults to `@,@-`.\n/// - `num_args = 0..=1` so the value form `--squash @,@-` also\n///   works without quoting.\n#[derive(Args, Debug, Clone, Default)]\npub struct SquashOption {\n    /// Squash SOURCE into TARGET [default: @,@-]\n    #[arg(\n        long,\n        value_name = \"SOURCE,TARGET\",\n        value_parser = SquashSpec::parse,\n        default_missing_value = \"@,@-\",\n        num_args = 0..=1\n    )]\n    pub squash: Option<SquashSpec>,\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn squash_parse_valid() {\n        let sq = SquashSpec::parse(\"@,@-\").unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(sq.source, \"@\");\n        assert_eq!(sq.target, \"@-\");\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn squash_parse_invalid() {\n        assert!(SquashSpec::parse(\"@\").is_err());\n        assert!(SquashSpec::parse(\",\").is_err());\n        assert!(SquashSpec::parse(\"@,\").is_err());\n        assert!(SquashSpec::parse(\",@-\").is_err());\n    }\n}\n"
}

> AGENT

Code unit done — fmt clean, clippy clean (`-D warnings`), all 378 tests pass (incl. `finalize::tests::*` and `options_flags::squash::tests::*`).

**Changes (one unit — `SquashSpec` moving across files + the version bump that rides with it):**

- `Cargo.toml` — `0.46.0` → `0.47.0`.
- `src/options_flags/squash.rs` *(new)* — `SquashSpec` + `SquashSpec::parse` (now `pub`, with `PartialEq, Eq`), a `SquashSpecParser` unit-struct `OptionParser` impl (leaf-convention discipline), and the `SquashOption` leaf. The leaf's `#[arg]` now wires `value_parser = SquashSpec::parse`, so the field type is `Option<SquashSpec>` and malformed `--squash` is rejected at parse time. The two `squash_parse_*` tests moved here.
- `src/options_flags/mod.rs` — `pub mod squash;`.
- `src/finalize.rs` — `FinalizeArgs.squash` is now `#[command(flatten)] pub squash: SquashOption`; deleted the local `SquashSpec` + impl; `TryFrom<&FinalizeArgs>` simplified (squash already parsed — only `--repo` canonicalization is fallible now); doc comments on `FinalizeParams`/`try_from` updated; tests updated (`args.squash` → `args.squash.squash`; `try_from_bad_squash` → `bad_squash`, now asserting a clap parse error since malformed `--squash` errs earlier; added a small `squash_at()` test helper).

One behavior change worth noting: `vc-x1 finalize --squash @` now fails during argument parsing instead of when `FinalizeParams` is built (strictly earlier/clearer error).

Please review. On approval I'll do the docs unit (`ARCHITECTURE.md`, `notes/chores-09.md`, `notes/todo.md`), then run the full pre-commit checklist and propose the commit title/body.

> DEVELOPER

I thinking this should be SquashArgs and SquashParams?

> DEVELOPER

I'm thinking we should use SquashFlag not SquashSpec?

> AGENT

`SquashSpec` is the **parsed value type**, not a leaf — so the `*Flag` / `*Option` suffix convention (which classifies *leaves* by value domain) doesn't apply to it. The parallel in the existing leaves:

| flag | leaf (`#[derive(Args)]`) | parsed value | parser |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--scope` | `ScopeOption` | `ScopeKind` | `ScopeParser` |
| `--repo` | `RepoOption` | `RepoSelector` | `RepoParser` |
| `--squash` | `SquashOption` | `SquashSpec` | `SquashSpecParser` |

And `--squash` is firmly in the **Option** (non-boolean) category by the README's own rule — it carries a `SOURCE,TARGET` revision pair, not a `bool`. The bare `--squash` form is just `num_args = 0..=1` + `default_missing_value = "@,@-"`; that's an Option with an optional value, not a Flag. Calling anything here `SquashFlag` would (a) mislabel the domain and (b) clash with the `*Flag` suffix reserved for boolean leaves like `DryRunFlag`.

So I'd keep the leaf as `SquashOption`. The only open naming question is the **value type** — `SquashSpec` parallels `ScopeKind` / `RepoSelector`. If `Spec` doesn't sit right, alternatives that read well: `SquashRevs`, `SquashPair`, `SquashFromInto`.

> DEVELOPER

Really we have to have a.squash.squash.xxx, where is that defined?

> AGENT

`a.squash` is the flattened leaf (`SquashOption`); the inner `.squash` is that leaf's one field. Two definitions:

`src/finalize.rs` — `FinalizeArgs`:
```rust
#[command(flatten)]
pub squash: SquashOption,        // a.squash : SquashOption
```

`src/options_flags/squash.rs` — the leaf:
```rust
pub struct SquashOption {
    #[arg(long, value_name = "SOURCE,TARGET", value_parser = SquashSpec::parse, ...)]
    pub squash: Option<SquashSpec>,   // a.squash.squash : Option<SquashSpec>
}
```

So `a.squash.squash` = "the `SquashOption` leaf flattened into the args" → "its `squash` field". This is the same shape every other leaf consumer uses today — `args.scope.scope` (`ScopeOption`), `args.repo.repo` (`RepoOption`), `args.provision.dry_run.dry_run` (`DryRunFlag`), `args.provision.push_retry.push_retries`. The `options_flags/README.md` calls it out explicitly: for single-field leaves, direct `args.<leaf>.<field>` read at the consumer is the accepted pattern.

The leaf-field name (`squash`) matches the flag name (`--squash`), which is why it doubles when the outer field is also named for the flag. The alternative — naming the leaf field something like `spec` — would break the convention that the leaf's field name *is* the long-flag name (clap derives `--squash` from the field identifier). Could pass `--squash` explicitly via `#[arg(long = "squash")]` and rename the field, but that diverges from how `scope`/`repo`/`dry_run` are written. I'd keep it consistent.

> DEVELOPER

Yeh, I hate it, can we call them "value" i.e. we're parsing the value side of an option?

> AGENT

Doable — the leaf becomes:

```rust
pub struct SquashOption {
    /// Squash SOURCE into TARGET [default: @,@-]
    #[arg(long = "squash", value_name = "SOURCE,TARGET",
          value_parser = SquashSpec::parse, default_missing_value = "@,@-", num_args = 0..=1)]
    pub value: Option<SquashSpec>,
}
```

…and the consumer reads `a.squash.value`. The `long = "squash"` is now required (clap would otherwise derive `--value` from the field name) — a small bit of boilerplate the current "field name *is* the flag name" convention avoids, but `value` reads clearly and the explicit `long = …` is idiomatic clap.

Two things to settle before I do it:

**Scope** — just the new `squash` leaf, or sweep all the single-field leaves (`scope`→`a.scope.value`, `repo`→`a.repo.value`, `dry_run`→`a.dry_run.value`, `private`, `account`, `config`, `use_template`)? Multi-field leaves like `push_retry` can't collapse to `value` and would stay as-is, so it'd be a "single-field leaves use `value`" rule. A full sweep touches `init.rs`, the leaf files, and their tests — bigger than this cycle; could be its own `0.47.1`.

**Docs** — either way, `options_flags/README.md` and `ARCHITECTURE.md` both currently state the field-name-equals-flag-name convention; that text needs updating to "single-field leaves name the field `value`; declare the flag via `#[arg(long = \"…\")]`".

45	Wire form is presentation; the category is the underlying domain.
46	
47	## Adding a new leaf
48	
49	1. Create `options_flags/<name>.rs` with a
50	   `#[derive(Args, Debug, Clone, Default)]` struct.
51	2. Pick the suffix per domain: `*Flag` (boolean) or `*Option(s)`
52	   (non-boolean).
53	3. Add `pub mod <name>;` to `mod.rs`.
54	4. If the leaf has explicit parsing logic, declare a unit-struct
55	   implementor of `FlagParser` (boolean domain) or `OptionParser`
56	   (non-boolean domain). Bare `Option<String>` leaves and
57	   presence/absence boolean leaves need no parser impl — clap
58	   handles them.
59	5. Add tests for any non-trivial parser/resolver logic.
60	6. Leaves do **not** implement a Bundle marker — bundles do.
61	
62	## Consuming an OF
63	
64	Default — flatten the leaf into your subcommand's `Args`:
65	
66	```rust
67	#[derive(Args)]
68	pub struct MyArgs {
69	    #[command(flatten)]
70	    pub config: ConfigOption,
71	    // ...
72	}
73	```
74	
75	Pattern A — when generic help doesn't fit:
76	
77	```rust
78	#[derive(Args)]
79	pub struct MyArgs {
80	    /// My subcommand-specific help for --config.
81	    #[arg(long = "config", value_name = "none|PATH",
82	          verbatim_doc_comment)]
83	    pub config: Option<String>,
84	    // ...
85	}
86	```
87	
88	A Pattern A consumer reuses the leaf's types (e.g. `ConfigKind`)
89	and parsers (e.g. `parse_config_kind`) but owns its own clap
90	attributes.
91	
92	## Consumer function shape
93	
94	Helper functions called by a subcommand body should accept the
95	relevant leaf type by reference rather than unpacking individual
96	fields at the call site:
97	
98	```rust
99	// Multi-field leaf — pass the whole leaf
100	fn run_retry(cmd: &str, args: &[&str], cwd: &Path,
101	             retry: &PushRetryOptions) -> Result<…> { … }
102	
103	run_retry("git", &["push", …], cwd, &args.provision.push_retry)?;
104	```
105	
106	This wins on:
107	- Readability — `&args.provision.push_retry` reads as a single
108	  unit; the function body works with `retry.push_retries` (no
109	  leaf-name doubling because the parameter name is the consumer's
110	  choice).
111	- Argument count — every multi-field leaf collapses N args into
112	  one ref, so chained helpers don't accumulate
113	  `clippy::too_many_arguments` warnings.
114	- Future-proofing — extending a leaf with another field is a
115	  zero-touch change at every call site.
116	
117	For **single-field leaves** (e.g. `DryRunFlag`, `PrivateFlag`),
118	direct read at the consumer site (`args.provision.dry_run.dry_run`)
119	is fine — wrapping a `bool` in `&LeafType` parameter doesn't earn
120	the indirection.
121	
122	## Marker traits
123	
124	Three Bundle markers — one per content classification. Bundles
```

Leaves do **not** implement a Bundle marker — bundles do."

693	## finalize subcommand-layer migration (0.46.0)
694	
695	Single-step cycle: bring `finalize` from "partial" to fully
696	on the subcommand-layer shape. It already had a clap-free
697	`FinalizeOpts` built via `FinalizeArgs::into_opts(log)`; this
698	cycle renames it to `FinalizeParams`, gives it a
699	`TryFrom<&FinalizeArgs>` boundary (fallible — `--squash`
700	parsing + `--repo` canonicalization), threads a `&Context`,
701	and moves the `--log` path onto `Context` (finalize is its
702	first non-`UserConfig` consumer). No behavior change.
703	
704	Decisions: kept the `From` / `TryFrom` split rather than
705	forcing `TryFrom` everywhere — `From` is the right trait when
706	the conversion is total (`init`), `TryFrom` when it isn't
707	(`finalize`). `--log` went on `Context` (resolved once at
708	startup, like `UserConfig`) rather than staying a
709	`FinalizeParams` field.
710	
711	### Edits
712	
713	- `Cargo.toml`: bump 0.45.0 → 0.46.0.
714	- `src/context.rs`: `Context` gains `pub log: Option<PathBuf>`;
715	  `Context::load()` → `Context::load(log: Option<PathBuf>)`;
716	  doc comments updated.
717	- `src/finalize.rs`: added module `//!` docstring (had none);
718	  `FinalizeOpts` → `FinalizeParams` (dropped its `log` field —
719	  moved to `Context`); `FinalizeArgs::into_opts(self, log)`
720	  replaced by `impl TryFrom<&FinalizeArgs> for FinalizeParams`;
721	  `finalize` / `detach` now `(ctx: &Context, params:
722	  &FinalizeParams)` and read `ctx.log`; `build_exec_args(params,
723	  log: Option<&Path>)`; `preflight` / `finalize_exec` /
724	  `log_plan` / `write_failure_marker` param renamed
725	  `opts` → `params`; tests updated, +2
726	  (`try_from_canonicalizes_repo`, `try_from_bad_squash`).
727	- `src/main.rs`: init arm passes `cli.log` to `Context::load`;
728	  finalize arm builds `Context::load(cli.log)` +
729	  `FinalizeParams::try_from(&finalize_args)` +
730	  `finalize::finalize(&ctx, &params)`.
731	- `src/test_helpers.rs`: `Context::load()` → `Context::load(None)`
732	  (the two fixture builders).
733	- `ARCHITECTURE.md`: Migration A table — `finalize` `partial` →
734	  `done (0.46.0)`; "Boundary conversion" bullet now states the
735	  `From` (total) vs `TryFrom` (fallible) rule; `Context` bullet
736	  mentions the `--log` path; subcommand-modules table updated;
737	  Migration A intro notes the planned `0.47.0-N` multi-step
738	  cycle for the remaining nine.

742	  `vc-x1 push --squash` will reuse it); `## Done` entry added;
743	  `[86]` ref added.

62	   best of both. Likely conflict surface is the bullet
63	   list under "How to apply".
64	1. **Symmetric `.vc-config.toml` schema.** Add `code = "/"`
65	   and `bot = "/.claude"` (workspace-root-relative paths) so
66	   both repos read from the same shape. Side detection walks
67	   up from cwd via existing `find_workspace_root`, then maps
68	   cwd-relative-to-root onto the configured `code` / `bot`

744	- `notes/chores-09.md`: this subsection.

746	## finalize Migration B — squash options_flags leaf (0.47.0)
747	
748	Single-step cycle. Migration B for `finalize`: lift the one
749	flag with a (planned) second consumer — `--squash` — into a
750	shared `options_flags/squash.rs` leaf. `--delay` / `--detach`
751	/ `--exec` / `--repo` / `--push` stay inline by design (no
752	second consumer yet); that's the documented terminal state for
753	finalize's Migration B, not unfinished work.
754	
755	The new leaf carries the `SquashSpec` value type (moved from
756	`finalize.rs`, now `pub`, `+ PartialEq, Eq`) and a
757	`SquashSpecParser` `OptionParser` impl (leaf-convention
758	discipline). The leaf's `#[arg]` wires `value_parser =
759	SquashSpec::parse`, so malformed `--squash` (e.g. `--squash @`)
760	is now rejected at argument-parse time rather than later in
761	`FinalizeParams::try_from` — the only behavior change, and a
762	strictly earlier/clearer error.
763	
764	Naming decision: the leaf's single field is named `value` (the
765	parsed value-side of the option), with the flag declared
766	explicitly via `#[arg(long = "squash")]`, so the consumer reads
767	`args.squash.value` instead of the doubled `args.squash.squash`.
768	This is a new convention for single-field leaves; the
769	pre-existing leaves (`scope`, `repo`, `dry_run`, …) still derive
770	the flag from the field name and are queued for a `0.47.1` sweep.
771	
772	### Edits
773	
774	- `Cargo.toml`: bump 0.46.0 → 0.47.0.
775	- `src/options_flags/squash.rs`: new — `SquashSpec` (+ `parse`,
776	  now `pub`; `+ PartialEq, Eq`), `SquashSpecParser`
777	  (`OptionParser`), `SquashOption` leaf (`value: Option<SquashSpec>`,
778	  `#[arg(long = "squash", value_parser = SquashSpec::parse, …)]`);
779	  the two `squash_parse_*` tests moved here.
780	- `src/options_flags/mod.rs`: `pub mod squash;`.
781	- `src/finalize.rs`: `FinalizeArgs.squash` → `#[command(flatten)]
782	  SquashOption`; removed the local `SquashSpec` + impl; `TryFrom`
783	  simplified (squash already parsed — only `--repo`
784	  canonicalization is fallible); doc comments on `FinalizeParams`
785	  / `try_from` updated; tests updated (`args.squash.value`;
786	  `try_from_bad_squash` → `bad_squash`, asserting a clap parse
787	  error; `squash_at()` test helper added).
788	- `src/options_flags/README.md`: "Adding a new leaf" gains the
789	  field-naming step (`value` for single-field leaves +
790	  `#[arg(long = "…")]`; multi-field keep descriptive names; note
791	  the pre-`value` leaves); "Consumer function shape" single-field
792	  example updated to `args.squash.value`.
793	- `ARCHITECTURE.md`: `squash` added to the leaves list; Migration
794	  B "State today" — `finalize` split out (`--squash` lifted; the
795	  rest stay inline); Migration A intro earmark `0.47.0-N` →
796	  `0.48.0-N`.

Starting the `0.48.0-N` cycle = step `-0`: bump to `0.48.0-0`, capture the plan in `notes/chores-09.md`, set up the `## In Progress` ladder in `notes/todo.md`, commit as a chore marker. Before I do that, here's the proposed cycle structure for your sign-off:

## 0.48.0-N — Migration A sweep (args → Context + Params)

Multi-step, **one step per subcommand**, same shape as `init` (0.44.0) / `finalize` (0.46.0): add an `XxxParams` flat struct + `impl From<&XxxArgs>` (or `TryFrom`, if the conversion is fallible) at the binary edge; change `pub fn x(args: &XxxArgs)` → `pub fn x(ctx: &Context, params: &XxxParams)`; update the `main.rs` dispatch arm (build `Context::load(cli.log)` + the params); update tests; flip the ARCHITECTURE.md Migration A table row.

**Ladder:**
- `0.48.0-0` — plan marker (version bump, chores subsection, todo `## In Progress` ladder).
- `0.48.0-1` — `symlink`
- `0.48.0-2` — `clone`
- `0.48.0-3` — `sync`
- `0.48.0-4` — `validate-desc`
- `0.48.0-5` — `fix-desc`

- `0.48.0` — close-out (drop suffix; todo In Progress → Done; Migration A table all-done).

**Order rationale:** smallest/simplest first (`symlink` ~16K, `clone` ~9K), `sync` next (~22K, has `integration_tests`), the `desc` pair, `push` (~54K) last.

**Not in this cycle:** `chid` / `desc` / `list` / `show` — they ride the separate "CommonArgs sweep" since their Migration A and B are entangled (per ARCHITECTURE.md / todo.md).

Out of scope for these ports (per ARCHITECTURE.md): typed errors, returned-outcomes-vs-`println!`, `ProgressSink`, and any `Context` fields beyond `UserConfig` + `--log`.
