```
191	## Test-module extraction (0.43.0)
192	
193	Multi-step cycle to extract `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` (and
194	`mod integration_tests` where present) from oversized
195	production files into sibling-submodule layout. Pure
196	mechanical reshape — no behavior change, no API change.
197	Each test still reaches private items via
198	`use super::*;`.
199	
200	### Goals
201	
202	- Shrink production files so the actual code is easier
203	  to navigate (`init.rs` is 2576 lines today, ~1093 of
204	  which are tests).
205	- Establish a layout shape that future cycles can keep
206	  using (further splitting into `cli.rs` / `ops.rs` per
207	  the subcommand-layer architecture, etc.).
208	- Done as one cycle so the four files end up
209	  consistent rather than half-converted across multiple
210	  cycles.
211	
212	### Per-file shape
213	
214	Non-mod.rs layout (Rust 2018+ idiom): the production
215	file keeps its top-level path; a sibling directory holds
216	children. `src/X.rs` and `src/X/` coexist — Rust resolves
217	`mod foo;` declared in `src/X.rs` to `src/X/foo.rs`.
218	
219	```
220	src/X.rs               ← production code (unchanged path)
221	src/X/
222	  tests.rs             ← moved from #[cfg(test)] mod tests
223	  integration_tests.rs ← moved from #[cfg(test)] mod
224	                         integration_tests (push, sync)
225	```
226	
227	In `src/X.rs` after extraction:
228	
229	```rust
230	// ...production code...
231	
232	#[cfg(test)]
233	mod tests;
234	
235	#[cfg(test)]
236	mod integration_tests;  // only for push, sync
237	```
238	
239	In `src/X/tests.rs`:
240	
241	```rust
242	//! Unit tests for the X module.
243	
244	use super::*;
245	// ...test fns...
246	```
247	
248	**Why non-mod.rs.** The `mod.rs` style names every
249	module's entry point identically — fuzzy finders show a
250	column of `mod.rs` rows that the reader must
251	disambiguate by directory. Multiple `tests.rs` files
252	don't have the same pain (the file is leaf content; the
253	directory tells the reader which module's tests they
254	are). Modern Rust (`cargo new`, RFC 2126) defaults to
255	non-mod.rs for exactly this reason.
256	
257	### Sub-step ladder
258	
259	Size-descending order (init has the most test bulk;
260	common is borderline):
261	
262	- 0.43.0-0 plan + version bump + this section +
263	  todo.md ladder
264	- 0.43.0-1 src/init.rs (kept) + src/init/tests.rs
265	  (~1093 test lines)
266	- 0.43.0-2 src/push.rs (kept) + src/push/{tests.rs,
267	  integration_tests.rs} (~784 test lines + integration
268	  block)
269	- 0.43.0-3 src/sync.rs (kept) + src/sync/{tests.rs,

271	  `#[cfg(test)]` blocks)

276	
277	### Order rationale
278	
279	`init` is the worked example: largest test bulk and the
280	file most-discussed in CLAUDE.md. Getting the pattern
281	right there sets the template for the others. The bot
282	thinks `common` is worth keeping for consistency but
283	reasonable to drop after seeing the first three land —
284	its 384 lines are below the threshold where extraction
285	visibly helps.
286	
287	### Per-substep contract
288	
289	Per `notes/substep-protocol.md`:
290	
291	- Run `cargo fmt`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D
292	  warnings`, `cargo test`, `cargo install --path .
293	  --locked`, retest before each sub-step commit.
294	- Bump `Cargo.toml` version at the start of each
295	  sub-step.
296	- Mark prior sub-step `(done)` and current `(current)`
297	  in todo.md ladder.
298	- Pair commits across both repos with ochid trailers.
299	
300	### Cycle exemption from per-file review
301	
302	Test-module extraction is exactly the "code moving
303	across files" refactor case CLAUDE.md
304	(`### Per-file review checkpoints > Exceptions`)
305	exempts. Each sub-step commits as one unit; no
306	checkpoint between original-file edit and new-file
307	creation.

316	  extraction TODO entry.

340	Per-file shape rationale above.

1	use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
2	
3	use clap::Args;
4	use log::{debug, info};
5	
6	/// What action the symlink operation needs to take.
7	#[derive(Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
8	pub enum SymlinkAction {
9	    /// Nothing exists at the symlink path — create it.
10	    Create,
11	    /// An existing symlink points somewhere else — caller must decide whether to replace.
12	    Replace { current_target: PathBuf },
13	    /// The symlink already points to the correct target — nothing to do.
14	    AlreadyCorrect,
15	}
16	
17	/// A planned symlink operation.
18	#[derive(Debug)]
19	pub struct SymLink {
20	    /// Absolute path to the target directory (what the symlink points to).
21	    pub abs_target: PathBuf,
22	    /// Full path of the symlink to create/replace.
23	    pub symlink_path: PathBuf,
24	    /// What action is needed.
25	    pub action: SymlinkAction,
26	}
27	
28	/// Encode a path the way Claude Code does: replace `/` and `.` with `-`.
29	pub fn encode_path(path: &str) -> String {
30	    path.replace(['/', '.'], "-")
31	}
32	
33	/// Read what exists at a path without following symlinks.
34	///
35	/// Returns:
36	/// - `None` — nothing exists
37	/// - `Some(None)` — exists but is not a symlink
38	/// - `Some(Some(target))` — symlink pointing to target
39	fn probe(path: &Path) -> Option<Option<PathBuf>> {
40	    match path.symlink_metadata() {
41	        Err(_) => None,
42	        Ok(meta) => {
43	            if meta.is_symlink() {
44	                Some(Some(std::fs::read_link(path).unwrap_or_default())) // OK: TOCTOU race after symlink_metadata; empty PathBuf forces recreate
45	            } else {
46	                Some(None)
47	            }
48	        }
49	    }
50	}
51	
52	impl SymLink {
53	    /// Plan a symlink operation by probing the filesystem.
54	    ///
55	    /// # Arguments
56	    /// * `cwd` — current working directory (used to derive the symlink name)
57	    /// * `target` — the directory the symlink should point to (resolved to absolute)
58	    /// * `symlink_dir` — parent directory for the symlink (e.g. `~/.claude/projects`)
59	    pub fn new(cwd: &Path, target: &Path, symlink_dir: &Path) -> Result<Self, String> {
60	        let abs_target = if target.is_absolute() {
61	            target.to_path_buf()
62	        } else {
63	            cwd.join(target)
64	        };
65	
66	        let cwd_str = cwd
67	            .to_str()
68	            .ok_or_else(|| "current directory path is not valid UTF-8".to_string())?;
69	        let symlink_name = encode_path(cwd_str);
70	        let symlink_path = symlink_dir.join(symlink_name);
71	
72	        let action = match probe(&symlink_path) {
73	            None => SymlinkAction::Create,
74	            Some(None) => {
75	                return Err(format!(
76	                    "'{}' exists and is not a symlink",
77	                    symlink_path.display()
78	                ));
79	            }
80	            Some(Some(current_target)) => {
81	                if current_target == abs_target {
82	                    SymlinkAction::AlreadyCorrect
83	                } else {
84	                    SymlinkAction::Replace { current_target }
85	                }
86	            }
87	        };
88	
89	        Ok(SymLink {
90	            abs_target,
91	            symlink_path,
92	            action,
93	        })
94	    }
95	
96	    /// Plan a symlink operation from pre-probed metadata (for testing).
97	    #[cfg(test)]
98	    pub fn with_meta(
99	        cwd: &Path,
100	        target: &Path,
101	        symlink_dir: &Path,
102	        symlink_meta: Option<Option<PathBuf>>,
103	    ) -> Result<Self, String> {
104	        let abs_target = if target.is_absolute() {
105	            target.to_path_buf()
106	        } else {
107	            cwd.join(target)
108	        };
109

112	            .ok_or_else(|| "current directory path is not valid UTF-8".to_string())?;
113	        let symlink_name = encode_path(cwd_str);
114	        let symlink_path = symlink_dir.join(symlink_name);
115	
116	        let action = match symlink_meta {
117	            None => SymlinkAction::Create,
118	            Some(None) => {
119	                return Err(format!(
120	                    "'{}' exists and is not a symlink",
121	                    symlink_path.display()
122	                ));
123	            }
124	            Some(Some(current_target)) => {
125	                if current_target == abs_target {
126	                    SymlinkAction::AlreadyCorrect
127	                } else {
128	                    SymlinkAction::Replace { current_target }
129	                }
130	            }
131	        };
132	
133	        Ok(SymLink {
134	            abs_target,
135	            symlink_path,
136	            action,
137	        })
138	    }
139	
140	    /// Execute the symlink operation: create the target dir if needed, create/replace the symlink.
141	    pub fn create(&self, create_target: bool) -> Result<(), String> {
142	        debug!(
143	            "symlink {} -> {}",
144	            self.symlink_path.display(),
145	            self.abs_target.display()
146	        );
147	
148	        if create_target && !self.abs_target.exists() {
149	            std::fs::create_dir_all(&self.abs_target).map_err(|e| {
150	                format!("cannot create target '{}': {e}", self.abs_target.display())
151	            })?;
152	        }
153	
154	        if let Some(parent) = self.symlink_path.parent() {
155	            std::fs::create_dir_all(parent).map_err(|e| {
156	                format!(
157	                    "cannot create symlink directory '{}': {e}",
158	                    parent.display()
159	                )
160	            })?;
161	        }
162	
163	        match &self.action {
164	            SymlinkAction::Create => {}
165	            SymlinkAction::Replace { .. } => {
166	                std::fs::remove_file(&self.symlink_path).map_err(|e| {
167	                    format!(
168	                        "cannot remove existing symlink '{}': {e}",
169	                        self.symlink_path.display()
170	                    )
171	                })?;
172	            }
173	            SymlinkAction::AlreadyCorrect => return Ok(()),
174	        }
175	
176	        #[cfg(unix)]
177	        std::os::unix::fs::symlink(&self.abs_target, &self.symlink_path).map_err(|e| {
178	            format!(
179	                "cannot create symlink '{}' -> '{}': {e}",
180	                self.symlink_path.display(),
181	                self.abs_target.display()
182	            )
183	        })?;
184	
185	        #[cfg(not(unix))]
186	        return Err("symlink creation is only supported on Unix".to_string());
187	
188	        Ok(())
189	    }
190	}
191	
192	/// `~/.claude/projects/` — the standard Claude Code symlink
193	/// directory. Shared default for `install` and the
194	/// `vc-x1 symlink` subcommand.
195	fn default_symlink_dir() -> Result<PathBuf, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
196	    let home =
197	        std::env::var("HOME").map_err(|_| "HOME environment variable not set".to_string())?;
198	    Ok(PathBuf::from(home).join(".claude").join("projects"))
199	}
200	
201	/// Install the standard Claude Code workspace symlink for `project_dir`.
202	///
203	/// Links `~/.claude/projects/<encoded>` → `<project_dir>/.claude`.
204	/// Silent — caller is expected to announce the action (if desired)
205	/// and to include the resulting `SymLink` in any final summary
206	/// output.
207	///
208	/// Shared by `vc-x1 clone --scope=code,bot` and `vc-x1 init
209	/// --scope=code,bot`. The full `vc-x1 symlink` subcommand below
210	/// can't directly call `install` — it has different
211	/// responsibilities (`--target` / `--symlink-dir` overrides,
212	/// replace prompts, `--list`, and `sl.create(true)` vs `false`)
213	/// — but both share `default_symlink_dir` for default-path
214	/// computation.
215	pub fn install(project_dir: &Path) -> Result<SymLink, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
216	    let sl = SymLink::new(project_dir, Path::new(".claude"), &default_symlink_dir()?)?;
217	    sl.create(false)?;
218	    Ok(sl)
219	}
220	
221	// -- Subcommand --
222	
223	#[derive(Args, Debug)]
224	pub struct SymlinkArgs {
225	    /// Directory to link to [default: .claude]
226	    #[arg(value_name = "TARGET")]
227	    pub target: Option<String>,
228	
229	    /// Directory for symlink [default: ~/.claude/projects]
230	    #[arg(long, value_name = "PATH")]
231	    pub symlink_dir: Option<PathBuf>,
232	
233	    /// List contents of symlinked directory after creation
234	    #[arg(short, long)]
235	    pub list: bool,
236	
237	    /// Replace existing symlink without prompting
238	    #[arg(short, long)]
239	    pub yes: bool,
240	}
241	
242	pub fn symlink(args: &SymlinkArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
243	    debug!("symlink: enter");
244	    let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;
245	
246	    let target = match &args.target {
247	        Some(t) => PathBuf::from(t),
248	        None => PathBuf::from(".claude"),
249	    };
250	
251	    let symlink_dir = match &args.symlink_dir {
252	        Some(d) => d.clone(),
253	        None => default_symlink_dir()?,
254	    };
255	
256	    let sl = SymLink::new(&cwd, &target, &symlink_dir)?;
257	
258	    // Handle interactive prompt for replacement
259	    if let SymlinkAction::Replace { ref current_target } = sl.action {
260	        info!(
261	            "Existing symlink: {} -> {}",
262	            sl.symlink_path.display(),
263	            current_target.display()
264	        );
265	        if !args.yes {
266	            let response = crate::common::prompt("Replace with new target? [y/N] ")?;
267	            if !response.eq_ignore_ascii_case("y") {
268	                return Err("aborted".into());
269	            }
270	        }
271	    }
272	
273	    if sl.action == SymlinkAction::AlreadyCorrect {
274	        info!(
275	            "Already correct: {} -> {}",
276	            sl.symlink_path.display(),
277	            sl.abs_target.display()
278	        );
279	    } else {
280	        sl.create(true)?;
281	        info!(
282	            "Created: {} -> {}",
283	            sl.symlink_path.display(),
284	            sl.abs_target.display()
285	        );
286	    }
287	
288	    if args.list {
289	        let abs_target = if target.is_absolute() {
290	            target
291	        } else {
292	            cwd.join(&target)
293	        };
294	        info!("");
295	        info!("Contents of {}:", abs_target.display());
296	        for entry in std::fs::read_dir(&abs_target)? {
297	            let entry = entry?;
298	            info!("  {}", entry.file_name().to_string_lossy());
299	        }
300	    }
301	
302	    debug!("symlink: exit");
303	    Ok(())
304	}

1	//! Flat plain-struct input to the init op.
2	//!
3	//! `InitParams` is the clap-free shape that `pub fn init` reads
4	//! from. Built `From<&InitArgs>` at the binary edge (main.rs);
5	//! callers without clap (tests, future TUI / library embedding)
6	//! construct it directly.
7	//!
8	//! Per `notes/chores-09.md > ## InitParams implementation
9	//! (0.44.0)`: domain types pass through (`RepoSelector`,
10	//! `ScopeKind`, `ConfigKind`, `PushRetryOptions`); leaf clap
11	//! wrappers do not.
12	
13	use crate::config::RepoSelector;
14	use crate::init::InitArgs;
15	use crate::options_flags::config::ConfigKind;
16	use crate::options_flags::push_retry::PushRetryOptions;
17	use crate::options_flags::scope::ScopeKind;
18	
19	/// Inputs to the init op, flat, owned, clap-free.
20	///
21	/// - `target`: the TARGET positional argument (URL, owner/name,
22	///   path, or bare NAME).
23	/// - `name`: optional `NAME` positional override.
24	/// - `account`: `--account` value (None ⇒ default account chain).
25	/// - `repo`: `--repo` value parsed into a `RepoSelector`.
26	/// - `scope`: `--scope` resolved (default `code,bot`).
27	/// - `private`: `--private`.
28	/// - `dry_run`: `--dry-run`.
29	/// - `push_retry`: `--push-retries` / `--push-retry-delay`.
30	/// - `use_template`: `--use-template` value.
31	/// - `config`: resolved `--config` value (None ⇒ canned write).
32	/// - `create_symlink`: whether `init` should create the
33	///   `~/.claude/projects/` symlink for dual-scope runs (production
34	///   path = true; test fixtures suppress with false).
35	pub struct InitParams {
36	    pub target: String,
37	    pub name: Option<String>,
38	    pub account: Option<String>,
39	    pub repo: Option<RepoSelector>,
40	    pub scope: ScopeKind,
41	    pub private: bool,
42	    pub dry_run: bool,
43	    pub push_retry: PushRetryOptions,
44	    pub use_template: Option<String>,
45	    pub config: Option<ConfigKind>,
46	    pub create_symlink: bool,
47	}
48	
49	impl From<&InitArgs> for InitParams {
50	    /// Convert clap-derived `InitArgs` into the flat `InitParams`.
51	    ///
52	    /// Production path: sets `create_symlink: true`. Tests that
53	    /// need `create_symlink: false` construct `InitParams`
54	    /// directly (or post-edit the field).
55	    fn from(a: &InitArgs) -> Self {
56	        Self {
57	            target: a.target.clone(),
58	            name: a.name.clone(),
59	            account: a.account.account.clone(),
60	            repo: a.repo.repo.clone(),
61	            scope: a.scope.scope,
62	            private: a.provision.private.private,
63	            dry_run: a.provision.dry_run.dry_run,
64	            push_retry: a.provision.push_retry.clone(),
65	            use_template: a.use_template.use_template.clone(),
66	            config: a.config.resolve(ConfigKind::None),
67	            create_symlink: true,
68	        }
69	    }
70	}

1	//! Shared platform handle passed to every subcommand operation.
2	//!
3	//! `Context` holds platform state that is the same across every
4	//! subcommand: today, the loaded `UserConfig` and the resolved
5	//! `--log` path. Built once at CLI startup and threaded through
6	//! to the subcommand layer.
7	//!
8	//! The shape is deliberately minimal — fields like project root,
9	//! progress sinks, etc. are added when a real consumer surfaces.
10	//! See `ARCHITECTURE.md` for the CLI-args vs subcommand
11	//! Context+Params layering rationale.
12	
13	use std::path::PathBuf;
14	
15	use crate::config::{self, UserConfig};
16	
17	/// Shared platform handle for subcommand operations.
18	///
19	/// - `user_config`: the loaded user config
20	///   (`~/.config/vc-x1/config.toml` or its discovered equivalent).
21	/// - `log`: the `--log` path, if given — where file-bound output
22	///   goes (and what a re-exec'd child is told to log to).
23	pub struct Context {
24	    pub user_config: UserConfig,
25	    pub log: Option<PathBuf>,
26	}
27	
28	impl Context {
29	    /// Build a `Context` by loading the user config from disk and
30	    /// taking the CLI-resolved `--log` path.
31	    pub fn load(log: Option<PathBuf>) -> Result<Self, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
32	        Ok(Self {
33	            user_config: config::load()?,
34	            log,
35	        })
36	    }

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/symlink.rs",
  "old_string": "// -- Subcommand --\n\n#[derive(Args, Debug)]\npub struct SymlinkArgs {\n    /// Directory to link to [default: .claude]\n    #[arg(value_name = \"TARGET\")]\n    pub target: Option<String>,\n\n    /// Directory for symlink [default: ~/.claude/projects]\n    #[arg(long, value_name = \"PATH\")]\n    pub symlink_dir: Option<PathBuf>,\n\n    /// List contents of symlinked directory after creation\n    #[arg(short, long)]\n    pub list: bool,\n\n    /// Replace existing symlink without prompting\n    #[arg(short, long)]\n    pub yes: bool,\n}\n\npub fn symlink(args: &SymlinkArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    debug!(\"symlink: enter\");\n    let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;\n\n    let target = match &args.target {\n        Some(t) => PathBuf::from(t),\n        None => PathBuf::from(\".claude\"),\n    };\n\n    let symlink_dir = match &args.symlink_dir {\n        Some(d) => d.clone(),\n        None => default_symlink_dir()?,\n    };\n\n    let sl = SymLink::new(&cwd, &target, &symlink_dir)?;\n\n    // Handle interactive prompt for replacement\n    if let SymlinkAction::Replace { ref current_target } = sl.action {\n        info!(\n            \"Existing symlink: {} -> {}\",\n            sl.symlink_path.display(),\n            current_target.display()\n        );\n        if !args.yes {\n            let response = crate::common::prompt(\"Replace with new target? [y/N] \")?;\n            if !response.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"y\") {\n                return Err(\"aborted\".into());\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    if sl.action == SymlinkAction::AlreadyCorrect {\n        info!(\n            \"Already correct: {} -> {}\",\n            sl.symlink_path.display(),\n            sl.abs_target.display()\n        );\n    } else {\n        sl.create(true)?;\n        info!(\n            \"Created: {} -> {}\",\n            sl.symlink_path.display(),\n            sl.abs_target.display()\n        );\n    }\n\n    if args.list {\n        let abs_target = if target.is_absolute() {\n            target\n        } else {\n            cwd.join(&target)\n        };",
  "new_string": "// -- Subcommand --\n\n#[derive(Args, Debug)]\npub struct SymlinkArgs {\n    /// Directory to link to [default: .claude]\n    #[arg(value_name = \"TARGET\")]\n    pub target: Option<String>,\n\n    /// Directory for symlink [default: ~/.claude/projects]\n    #[arg(long, value_name = \"PATH\")]\n    pub symlink_dir: Option<PathBuf>,\n\n    /// List contents of symlinked directory after creation\n    #[arg(short, long)]\n    pub list: bool,\n\n    /// Replace existing symlink without prompting\n    #[arg(short, long)]\n    pub yes: bool,\n}\n\n/// Inputs to the symlink op, flat, owned, clap-free.\n///\n/// - `target`: the `TARGET` positional (None ⇒ `.claude`).\n/// - `symlink_dir`: `--symlink-dir` override (None ⇒\n///   `~/.claude/projects`).\n/// - `list`: `-l` / `--list` — list the linked dir's contents\n///   afterward.\n/// - `yes`: `-y` / `--yes` — replace an existing symlink without\n///   prompting.\npub struct SymlinkParams {\n    pub target: Option<String>,\n    pub symlink_dir: Option<PathBuf>,\n    pub list: bool,\n    pub yes: bool,\n}\n\nimpl From<&SymlinkArgs> for SymlinkParams {\n    /// Convert clap-derived `SymlinkArgs` into the flat\n    /// `SymlinkParams` (total — every field copies straight over).\n    fn from(a: &SymlinkArgs) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            target: a.target.clone(),\n            symlink_dir: a.symlink_dir.clone(),\n            list: a.list,\n            yes: a.yes,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Run the `symlink` subcommand: create — or replace, with a\n/// prompt unless `--yes` — the `~/.claude/projects/<encoded-cwd>`\n/// symlink for the current directory.\n///\n/// `ctx` is unused today (symlink reads neither user config nor\n/// the `--log` path); it's present for the uniform subcommand-layer\n/// signature.\npub fn symlink(\n    _ctx: &Context,\n    params: &SymlinkParams,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    debug!(\"symlink: enter\");\n    let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;\n\n    let target = match &params.target {\n        Some(t) => PathBuf::from(t),\n        None => PathBuf::from(\".claude\"),\n    };\n\n    let symlink_dir = match &params.symlink_dir {\n        Some(d) => d.clone(),\n        None => default_symlink_dir()?,\n    };\n\n    let sl = SymLink::new(&cwd, &target, &symlink_dir)?;\n\n    // Handle interactive prompt for replacement\n    if let SymlinkAction::Replace { ref current_target } = sl.action {\n        info!(\n            \"Existing symlink: {} -> {}\",\n            sl.symlink_path.display(),\n            current_target.display()\n        );\n        if !params.yes {\n            let response = crate::common::prompt(\"Replace with new target? [y/N] \")?;\n            if !response.eq_ignore_ascii_case(\"y\") {\n                return Err(\"aborted\".into());\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    if sl.action == SymlinkAction::AlreadyCorrect {\n        info!(\n            \"Already correct: {} -> {}\",\n            sl.symlink_path.display(),\n            sl.abs_target.display()\n        );\n    } else {\n        sl.create(true)?;\n        info!(\n            \"Created: {} -> {}\",\n            sl.symlink_path.display(),\n            sl.abs_target.display()\n        );\n    }\n\n    if params.list {\n        let abs_target = if target.is_absolute() {\n            target\n        } else {\n            cwd.join(&target)\n        };"
}

133	  single-repo mode) and `parse_scope`.
134	
135	Subcommand modules — each holds an `XxxArgs` `#[derive(Args)]`
136	struct and a `pub fn x(...)` entrypoint:
137	
138	| Module | Subcommand | Notes |
139	| --- | --- | --- |
140	| `chid.rs` | `chid` | flattens `CommonArgs` |
141	| `desc.rs` (+ `desc_helpers.rs`) | `desc` | flattens `CommonArgs` |
142	| `list.rs` | `list` | flattens `CommonArgs` |
143	| `show.rs` | `show` | flattens `CommonArgs` |
144	| `validate_desc.rs` | `validate-desc` | |
145	| `fix_desc.rs` | `fix-desc` | |
146	| `clone.rs` | `clone` | |
147	| `init.rs` (+ `init/params.rs`) | `init` | subcommand-layer worked example (0.44.0) |
148	| `symlink.rs` | `symlink` | |
149	| `sync.rs` | `sync` | |
150	| `finalize.rs` | `finalize` | migrated (0.46.0) — `finalize(&Context, &FinalizeParams)`, `TryFrom<&FinalizeArgs>` |
151	| `push.rs` | `push` | resumable state machine |
152	
153	Support:
154	
155	- `src/repo_utils.rs`, `src/url.rs`, `src/toml_simple.rs`,
156	  `src/logging.rs` — jj/repo helpers, URL parsing, a minimal
157	  TOML reader, the CLI logger.
158	- `src/test_helpers.rs`, `src/test_tmp_root.rs` —
159	  `#[cfg(test)]` fixtures (`Fixture` / `FixturePor`) and
160	  tempdir resolution. Subcommand modules also carry their
161	  own `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` (and `integration_tests` for
162	  `sync` / `push`).
163	
164	## Subcommand model
165	
166	Adding a subcommand `x`:
167	
168	1. Create `src/x.rs` with `pub struct XxxArgs`
169	   (`#[derive(Args)]`), composing leaves from
170	   `src/options_flags/` where one exists.
171	2. Add a variant `X(x::XxxArgs)` to `Commands` in
172	   `src/main.rs` with a `///` summary (and
173	   `#[command(long_about = …)]` for the long form).
174	3. Add a dispatch arm in `main()` calling `x::x(&args)` (or,
175	   once ported, `x::x(&ctx, &params)`).
176	4. Doc comments on `#[arg(...)]` fields drive `--help`; add
177	   `#[arg(verbatim_doc_comment, …)]` on any field whose doc
178	   comment uses bullets (clap otherwise reflows them into
179	   prose).
180	5. Dynamic value completion (`--account=<TAB>`) is an
181	   `ArgValueCompleter` attached to the leaf in
182	   `src/options_flags/` — completion is a clap-aware concern
183	   and stays at this layer.
184	
185	## Migration A — args → Context + Params
186	
187	Port each subcommand's `pub fn x(args: &XxxArgs)` to
188	`pub fn x(ctx: &Context, params: &XxxParams)`, adding an
189	`XxxParams` flat struct + a `From` (or `TryFrom`, if the
190	conversion is fallible) at the binary edge. `init` (0.44.0)
191	and `finalize` (0.46.0) are done; the rest follow the same
192	shape. The live checklist is the "Subcommand layer / CLI
193	decoupling" item in [`notes/todo.md`](notes/todo.md).
194	
195	| Subcommand | Status |
196	| --- | --- |
197	| `init` | done (0.44.0) — `init(&Context, &InitParams)`; `From<&InitArgs>` |
198	| `finalize` | done (0.46.0) — `finalize(&Context, &FinalizeParams)`; `TryFrom<&FinalizeArgs>` (fallible boundary); `--log` moved onto `Context` |
199	| `sync` | not started |
200	| `chid` | not started |
201	| `desc` | not started |
202	| `list` | not started |
203	| `show` | not started |
204	| `validate-desc` | not started |
205	| `fix-desc` | not started |
206	| `clone` | not started |
207	| `push` | not started |
208	| `symlink` | not started |
209	
210	The remaining nine are planned as one multi-step cycle
211	(`0.48.0-N`), one step per subcommand (`chid`/`desc`/`list`/
212	`show` ride with the separate "CommonArgs sweep" since their
213	Migration A and B are entangled).
214	
215	Out of scope for the early ports (deferred until a real
216	consumer surfaces): typed errors, returned outcomes vs
217	`println!`, the `ProgressSink`, and `Context` fields beyond

- `src/symlink.rs` — added the missing `//!` module docstring; new `SymlinkParams` flat struct (`target` / `symlink_dir` / `list` / `yes`) + `impl From<&SymlinkArgs>` (total); `pub fn symlink(args: &SymlinkArgs)` → `pub fn symlink(_ctx: &Context, params: &SymlinkParams)` (body reads `params.*`; `ctx` unused — uniform-signature placeholder, noted in its doc comment); added the missing `///` on `symlink()`. Tests untouched (they exercise `SymLink` / `encode_path` / `probe`, not the subcommand fn).
- `src/main.rs` — `Symlink` dispatch arm now builds `Context::load(cli.log)` + `SymlinkParams::from(&symlink_args)`, same shape as the `init` / `finalize` arms.
- `ARCHITECTURE.md` — `symlink` row → `done (0.48.0-1)` in the Migration A table and the subcommand-modules table; Migration A intro lists `symlink` among the done ports.

No `notes/README.md` change — the `symlink` CLI surface is unchanged.

1	//! `vc-x1 clone` — clone a repo (URL or local path) into a workspace.
2	//!
3	//! - `--scope=code,bot` (default): dual-repo layout — clones code,
4	//!   derives bot source (`<source>.claude`), clones bot into
5	//!   `<target>/.claude`, creates the Claude Code symlink. Both
6	//!   sides must succeed.
7	//! - `--scope=por`: single repo into `<target>`. No `.claude/`,
8	//!   no symlink.
9	//!
10	//! TARGET shapes (all routed through `parse_target`): URL,
11	//! `owner/name` shorthand, or a local path (`./X`, `/X`, `~/X`,
12	//! `.`, `..`). Path-form is symmetric with `git clone /local/bare.git`
13	//! — useful for fixtures and CI scratch dirs.
14	//!
15	//! `clone_one` and `clone_dual` are `pub(crate)` so init's `-3`
16	//! reshape can reuse them for the "URL exists → clone" preflight
17	//! path (per `notes/chores-08.md > init + clone redesign`).
18	
19	use std::path::Path;
20	
21	use clap::Args;
22	use log::info;
23	
24	use crate::common::run;
25	use crate::options_flags::scope::{ScopeKind, parse_scope_kind};
26	use crate::symlink;
27	use crate::url::{Target, derive_name, derive_session_url, parse_target, resolve_url};
28	
29	/// CLI args for `vc-x1 clone`.
30	#[derive(Args, Debug)]
31	pub struct CloneArgs {
32	    /// Source to clone — URL, owner/name shorthand, or local path.
33	    ///
34	    /// - URL: `git@host:owner/name(.git)?`, `https://...(.git)?`
35	    /// - owner/name shorthand: resolves to
36	    ///   `git@github.com:owner/name.git`
37	    /// - Local path: `./X`, `../X`, `/X`, `~/X`, `~`, `.`, `..`
38	    ///   (passed directly to `git clone`)
39	    #[arg(value_name = "TARGET", verbatim_doc_comment)]
40	    pub target: String,
41	
42	    /// Destination dir name in cwd [default: derived from TARGET]
43	    #[arg(value_name = "NAME")]
44	    pub name: Option<String>,
45	
46	    /// What to clone: `code,bot` (dual, default) or `por` (single).
47	    #[arg(
48	        long,
49	        short,
50	        value_name = "SCOPE",
51	        value_parser = parse_scope_kind,
52	        default_value = "code,bot"
53	    )]
54	    pub scope: ScopeKind,
55	
56	    /// Dry run — show what would be done without executing.
57	    #[arg(long)]
58	    pub dry_run: bool,
59	}
60	
61	/// Top-level clone driver.
62	///
63	/// - Resolves TARGET to a concrete clone source (URL or path).
64	/// - Determines destination dir name (`[NAME]` override or
65	///   `derive_name(TARGET)`).
66	/// - Pre-checks target dir doesn't exist.
67	/// - Dispatches to `clone_one` (POR) or `clone_dual` (code,bot).
68	pub fn clone_repo(args: &CloneArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
69	    log::debug!("clone: enter");
70	
71	    let parsed = parse_target(&args.target)?;
72	    let source = match parsed {
73	        Target::Url(u) => u,
74	        Target::OwnerName(o, n) => resolve_url(&format!("{o}/{n}")),
75	        Target::Path(p) => p.to_str().ok_or("path is not valid UTF-8")?.to_string(),
76	        Target::BareName(n) => {
77	            return Err(format!(
78	                "'{n}' is a bare name — clone has no config-driven defaults; \
79	                 use 'owner/{n}', a full URL, or './{n}' for a local path"
80	            )
81	            .into());
82	        }
83	    };
84	
85	    let name = match &args.name {
86	        Some(n) => n.clone(),
87	        None => derive_name(&args.target)?,
88	    };
89	    let parent_dir = std::env::current_dir()?;
90	    let project_dir = parent_dir.join(&name);
91	
92	    if project_dir.exists() {
93	        return Err(format!("'{}' already exists", project_dir.display()).into());
94	    }
95	
96	    if args.dry_run {
97	        info!("Dry run — would execute:");
98	        match args.scope {
99	            ScopeKind::Por => {
100	                info!("  1. jj git clone --colocate {source} {name}");
101	            }
102	            ScopeKind::CodeBot => {
103	                let session_source = derive_session_url(&source);
104	                info!("  1. jj git clone --colocate {source} {name}");
105	                info!("  2. jj git clone --colocate {session_source} {name}/.claude");
106	                info!("  3. Create Claude Code symlink");
107	            }
108	        }
109	        return Ok(());
110	    }
111	
112	    run("jj", &["--version"], Path::new(".")).map_err(|_| "jj is not installed")?;
113	
114	    match args.scope {
115	        ScopeKind::Por => {
116	            clone_one(&source, &project_dir, &parent_dir)?;
117	            info!("");
118	            info!("Done! Project cloned to {}", project_dir.display());
119	            info!("  Code repo: {}", project_dir.display());
120	        }
121	        ScopeKind::CodeBot => clone_dual(&source, &project_dir, &parent_dir)?,
122	    }
123	
124	    log::debug!("clone: exit");
125	    Ok(())
126	}
127	
128	/// Clone a single repo via `jj git clone --colocate` and verify
129	/// bookmark tracking.
130	///
131	/// - `source` is the clone source (URL or local path).
132	/// - `target_dir` is the destination working repo location.
133	/// - `parent_dir` is the cwd for the `jj git clone` subprocess.
134	///
135	/// `jj git clone --colocate` does git clone + jj init + automatic
136	/// bookmark tracking in one step (unlike colocate-after-bare-git-clone,
137	/// which needs explicit `jj bookmark track`). The `verify_tracking`
138	/// call asserts the post-clone state matches expectations.
139	pub(crate) fn clone_one(
140	    source: &str,
141	    target_dir: &Path,
142	    parent_dir: &Path,
143	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
144	    let target_str = target_dir
145	        .to_str()
146	        .ok_or("target path is not valid UTF-8")?;
147	    info!("Cloning {source} → {target_str}...");
148	    run(
149	        "jj",
150	        &["git", "clone", "--colocate", source, target_str],
151	        parent_dir,
152	    )?;
153	    crate::common::verify_tracking(target_dir, "main")?;
154	    Ok(())
155	}
156	
157	/// Orchestrate a dual-repo clone: code via `clone_one`, bot via
158	/// `clone_one` (no graceful skip — `--scope=code,bot` requires
159	/// both sides; users who want code-only should use `--scope=por`),
160	/// then create the Claude Code symlink.
161	pub(crate) fn clone_dual(
162	    code_source: &str,
163	    target_dir: &Path,
164	    parent_dir: &Path,
165	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
166	    let session_source = derive_session_url(code_source);
167	    let session_dir = target_dir.join(".claude");
168	
169	    clone_one(code_source, target_dir, parent_dir)?;
170	    clone_one(&session_source, &session_dir, target_dir)?;
171	    info!("Creating Claude Code symlink...");
172	    let sl = symlink::install(target_dir)?;
173	
174	    info!("");
175	    info!("Done! Project cloned to {}", target_dir.display());
176	    info!("  Code repo:    {}", target_dir.display());
177	    info!("  Session repo: {}", session_dir.display());
178	    info!(
179	        "  Symlink:      {} -> {}",
180	        sl.symlink_path.display(),
181	        sl.abs_target.display()
182	    );
183	
184	    Ok(())
185	}
186

208	        assert_eq!(args.target, "owner/repo");

210	        assert_eq!(args.scope, ScopeKind::CodeBot);
211	        assert!(!args.dry_run);
212	    }

1	use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
2	use std::process::Command;
3	
4	use clap::Args;
5	use log::{LevelFilter, debug, info, warn};
6	
7	use crate::common::{default_scope, find_workspace_root, run, scope_to_repos};
8	use crate::scope::{Scope, parse_scope};
9	
10	/// Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes.
11	///
12	/// Default is `--check`: fetch + classify + report, **error** if any
13	/// repo is `behind` or `diverged`. Pass `--no-check` to actually
14	/// rebase / fast-forward. On any failure the starting state of every
15	/// repo is restored via `jj op restore`.
16	///
17	/// Scripts and automation should pass `--check` or `--no-check`
18	/// explicitly rather than relying on the default — defaults can shift,
19	/// explicit flags lock in the contract.
20	///
21	/// Repo set is resolved from `--scope`:
22	///
23	/// - Explicit `--scope=code|bot|code,bot|<path>` — keyword forms
24	///   resolve via the project root's `.vc-config.toml`; the path
25	///   form syncs that one repo (single-repo mode).
26	/// - No `--scope` — workspace-default scope:
27	///   - dual workspace (`.vc-config.toml` with `other-repo`) → `code,bot`
28	///   - single-repo workspace (`.vc-config.toml`, no `other-repo`) → `code`
29	///   - POR (no `.vc-config.toml`) → cwd as a single-repo target
30	#[derive(Args, Debug)]
31	pub struct SyncArgs {
32	    /// Verify only — fetch + classify; error if any repo needs action.
33	    /// This is the default; pass explicitly in scripts.
34	    #[arg(long, conflicts_with = "no_check")]
35	    pub check: bool,
36	
37	    /// Apply — fetch + classify, then rebase / fast-forward as needed.
38	    #[arg(long, conflicts_with = "check")]
39	    pub no_check: bool,
40	
41	    /// Suppress all informational output (exit code signals result)
42	    #[arg(short, long)]
43	    pub quiet: bool,
44	
45	    /// Bookmark to sync in each repo
46	    #[arg(long, default_value = "main")]
47	    pub bookmark: String,
48	
49	    /// Remote to sync against
50	    #[arg(long, default_value = "origin")]
51	    pub remote: String,
52	
53	    /// Which repo(s) of the workspace to sync.
54	    ///
55	    /// `SCOPE=code|bot|code,bot|<path>`:
56	    ///
57	    /// - `code` — sync only the app repo.
58	    /// - `bot` — sync only the bot repo (errors if no bot repo
59	    ///   is configured).
60	    /// - `code,bot` — sync both repos.
61	    /// - `<path>` (`./X`, `/X`, `~/X`) — single-repo mode; sync
62	    ///   only that repo, ignoring `.vc-config.toml`.
63	    ///
64	    /// Default depends on workspace state: dual workspace →
65	    /// `code,bot`; single-repo workspace → `code`; POR → cwd.
66	    #[arg(
67	        short = 's',
68	        long,
69	        value_name = "SCOPE",
70	        value_parser = parse_scope,
71	        verbatim_doc_comment
72	    )]
73	    pub scope: Option<Scope>,
74	}
75	
76	/// Resolve `args` into the concrete repo list `sync_repos` operates on.
77	///
78	/// Explicit `--scope` wins; otherwise resolves via `default_scope`
79	/// against the discovered workspace root and cwd.
80	fn resolve_args_to_repos(args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
81	    let workspace_root = find_workspace_root();
82	    let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;
83	    let scope = match &args.scope {
84	        Some(s) => s.clone(),
85	        None => default_scope(workspace_root.as_deref(), &cwd),
86	    };
87	    scope_to_repos(&scope, workspace_root.as_deref())
88	}
89	
90	/// Relationship between a local bookmark and its remote counterpart.
91	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
92	pub enum State {
93	    /// Local and remote point at the same commit.
94	    UpToDate,
95	    /// Local is a strict ancestor of remote — fast-forward possible.
96	    Behind { local: String, remote: String },
97	    /// Remote is a strict ancestor of local — nothing to pull in.
98	    Ahead { local: String, remote: String },
99	    /// Neither is an ancestor of the other — needs rebase.
100	    Diverged { local: String, remote: String },
101	    /// The bookmark has no `@<remote>` counterpart.
102	    NoRemote,
103	}
104	
105	/// Per-repo context accumulated between the snapshot and action phases.
106	#[derive(Debug)]
107	struct RepoCtx {
108	    path: PathBuf,
109	    #[allow(dead_code)]
110	    op_id: String,
111	    state: State,
112	}
113	
114	/// CLI entry point for the `sync` subcommand.
115	///
116	/// Thin wrapper over `sync_repos` that resolves the `-R` flag into a
117	/// concrete repo list (falling back to the dual-repo default) and
118	/// forwards the rest of the args. Tests call `sync_repos` directly
119	/// with absolute fixture paths.
120	///
121	/// When `--quiet` is set, the global log filter is temporarily clamped
122	/// to `Warn` for the duration of the call and restored on return, so
123	/// `info!` calls throughout sync (plus any subprocess-stderr routed
124	/// through `common::run`) go dark. Errors still surface at `Warn` /
125	/// `Error` so script callers don't lose diagnostics.
126	pub fn sync(args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
127	    let repos = resolve_args_to_repos(args)?;
128	    if args.quiet {
129	        let prev = log::max_level();
130	        log::set_max_level(LevelFilter::Warn);
131	        let result = sync_repos(&repos, args);
132	        log::set_max_level(prev);
133	        result
134	    } else {
135	        sync_repos(&repos, args)
136	    }
137	}
138	
139	/// Sync the given repos against their remotes.
140	///
141	/// Orchestrates the full flow: pre-flight clean-check on every repo,
142	/// snapshot each repo's current op id, then hand off to `run_plan` for
143	/// fetch + classify + (optional) act. On any error, revert every repo
144	/// to its snapshot op via `jj op restore` so the caller sees an atomic
145	/// "either it all went through or nothing changed" outcome.
146	///
147	/// Paths may be relative (resolved against the process cwd) or
148	/// absolute. Tests use absolute tempdir paths to avoid cwd dependence
149	/// under parallel `cargo test`.
150	pub fn sync_repos(repos: &[PathBuf], args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
151	    debug!(
152	        "sync: enter (no_check={}, bookmark={}, remote={})",
153	        args.no_check, args.bookmark, args.remote
154	    );
155	
156	    // Preflight: verify bookmark tracking on every repo before any
157	    // fetch/rebase. Implements "Non-tracking-remote bookmark detection"
158	    // — design at:
159	    //   https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/blob/main/notes/chores-06.md#non-tracking-remote-bookmark-detection-design
160	    for repo in repos {
161	        crate::common::verify_tracking(repo, &args.bookmark)?;
162	    }
163	
164	    let mut snapshots: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = Vec::new();
165	    for repo in repos {
166	        let op_id = current_op_id(repo)?;
167	        debug!("{}: op snapshot = {op_id}", repo.display());
168	        snapshots.push((repo.clone(), op_id));
169	    }
170	
171	    let result = run_plan(&snapshots, args);
172	
173	    if let Err(e) = &result {
174	        warn!("sync failed: {e}");
175	        warn!("reverting all repos to starting state...");
176	        for (repo, op_id) in &snapshots {
177	            match op_restore(repo, op_id) {
178	                Ok(()) => info!("  {}: reverted to op {op_id}", repo.display()),
179	                Err(re) => warn!("  {}: revert failed: {re}", repo.display()),
180	            }
181	        }
182	    }
183	
184	    debug!("sync: exit");
185	    result
186	}
187	
188	/// Fetch and classify each repo, then act (or not, in dry-run).
189	///
190	/// Returns `Err` on the first failure so the caller can trigger the
191	/// cross-repo revert; partial success across repos is *not* a valid
192	/// end state for this command.
193	fn run_plan(
194	    snapshots: &[(PathBuf, String)],
195	    args: &SyncArgs,
196	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
197	    // Phase 1 — fetch + classify silently.
198	    //
199	    // Each repo's fetch stderr is captured (rather than streamed to
200	    // `info!` via `common::run`) so we can decide after classification
201	    // whether to surface it. `jj git fetch`'s routine "Nothing
202	    // changed." chatter is the main thing we're suppressing here —
203	    // if nothing needs action, the user shouldn't see it.
204	    let mut fetched: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = Vec::new();
205	    let mut ctxs: Vec<RepoCtx> = Vec::new();
206	    for (repo, op_id) in snapshots {
207	        let stderr = fetch_silent(repo, &args.remote)?;
208	        fetched.push((repo.clone(), stderr));
209	        let state = classify(repo, &args.bookmark, &args.remote)?;
210	        ctxs.push(RepoCtx {
211	            path: repo.clone(),
212	            op_id: op_id.clone(),
213	            state,
214	        });
215	    }
216	
217	    let any_action_needed = ctxs
218	        .iter()
219	        .any(|c| matches!(c.state, State::Behind { .. } | State::Diverged { .. }));
220	
221	    // Phase 2 — emit status. `--quiet` is enforced globally via the
222	    // log-level clamp in `sync()`, so these `info!` calls are already
223	    // suppressed in scripts; we just shape the output here.
224	    if !any_action_needed {
225	        let n = ctxs.len();
226	        let noun = if n == 1 { "repo" } else { "repos" };
227	        info!("sync: {n} {noun}, all bookmarks up-to-date");
228	    } else {
229	        for (repo, stderr) in &fetched {
230	            info!("{}: fetch {}", repo.display(), args.remote);
231	            for line in stderr.lines() {
232	                info!("{line}");
233	            }
234	        }
235	        for ctx in &ctxs {
236	            log_state(&ctx.path, &ctx.state);
237	        }
238	    }
239	
240	    // Phase 3 — act (subprocess output streams through as usual).
241	    // `act_on_state` and `ensure_at_on_main` short-circuit when
242	    // `args.no_check` is false, so check mode is a true no-op here.
243	    for ctx in &ctxs {
244	        act_on_state(ctx, args)?;
245	        ensure_at_on_main(&ctx.path, &args.bookmark, args.no_check)?;
246	    }
247	
248	    // Phase 4 — check mode is fatal when action would be needed.
249	    // Apply mode (`--no-check`) ran the action above and is done.
250	    if !args.no_check && any_action_needed {
251	        let n_action = ctxs
252	            .iter()
253	            .filter(|c| matches!(c.state, State::Behind { .. } | State::Diverged { .. }))
254	            .count();
255	        let noun = if n_action == 1 { "repo" } else { "repos" };
256	        return Err(format!(
257	            "sync: {n_action} {noun} need action (see above) — \
258	             resolve with `vc-x1 sync --no-check` and re-run"
259	        )
260	        .into());
261	    }
262	    Ok(())
263	}
264	
265	/// Fetch `repo` from `remote` without streaming subprocess output to
266	/// `info!`.
267	///
268	/// Mirrors what `common::run` would do, but returns stderr to the
269	/// caller so the caller can decide whether to surface it (verbose /
270	/// action case) or drop it (clean case). Stdout is dropped — `jj git
271	/// fetch` doesn't use it. Failure carries stderr in the error message.
272	fn fetch_silent(repo: &Path, remote: &str) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
273	    debug!("$ jj git fetch --remote {remote} -R {}", repo.display());
274	    let output = Command::new("jj")
275	        .args(["git", "fetch", "--remote", remote, "-R", &repo_str(repo)])
276	        .output()
277	        .map_err(|e| format!("failed to run jj git fetch: {e}"))?;
278	    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).trim().to_string();
279	    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string();
280	    if !stdout.is_empty() {
281	        debug!("  {stdout}");
282	    }
283	    if !output.status.success() {
284	        return Err(format!("jj git fetch -R {} failed: {stderr}", repo.display()).into());
285	    }
286	    Ok(stderr)
287	}
288	
289	/// Ensure `@` is a descendant of `bookmark`, rebasing if not.
290	///
291	/// `jj git fetch` fast-forwards a tracked local bookmark to the remote
292	/// tip when local is a strict ancestor, but leaves `@` behind — still
293	/// parented on the *pre-fetch* bookmark commit. The `.claude` repo is
294	/// the motivating case: trailing session writes (e.g. `/exit`'s jsonl
295	/// tail) sit in `@`, and without this step they'd end up orphaned on a
296	/// stale branch when the remote advanced.
297	///
298	/// Skipped when `main::@` is already non-empty (i.e., `@` is already
299	/// reachable from `bookmark`). On `--no-check`, runs
300	/// `jj rebase -b @ -d <bookmark>` which carries all commits between
301	/// the old parent and `@` forward, then checks `conflicts()` — any
302	/// conflict in this step trips the outer revert the same way a
303	/// conflicted `act_on_state` rebase would.
304	fn ensure_at_on_main(
305	    repo: &Path,
306	    bookmark: &str,
307	    apply: bool,
308	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
309	    if revset_nonempty(repo, &format!("{bookmark}::@"))? {
310	        return Ok(());
311	    }
312	    info!("{}: rebasing @ onto '{bookmark}'", repo.display());
313	    if apply {
314	        run(
315	            "jj",
316	            &["rebase", "-b", "@", "-d", bookmark, "-R", &repo_str(repo)],
317	            Path::new("."),
318	        )?;
319	        if has_conflicts(repo)? {
320	            return Err(format!(
321	                "{}: rebase of @ onto '{bookmark}' produced conflicts",
322	                repo.display()
323	            )
324	            .into());
325	        }
326	    }
327	    Ok(())
328	}
329	
330	/// Perform the mutation corresponding to `ctx.state` when `--no-check`.
331	///
332	/// - `UpToDate` / `Ahead` / `NoRemote` → no-op (and no output, the state
333	///   was already logged by `log_state`).
334	/// - `Behind` → `jj bookmark set <b> -r <b>@<remote>` to fast-forward.
335	/// - `Diverged` → `jj rebase -b <b> -d <b>@<remote>`, then probe
336	///   `conflicts()`. A non-empty result means the rebase produced
337	///   conflicted commits; return `Err` so the outer revert restores the
338	///   pre-fetch state.
339	fn act_on_state(ctx: &RepoCtx, args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
340	    let repo = &ctx.path;
341	    let remote_rev = format!("{}@{}", args.bookmark, args.remote);
342	    match &ctx.state {
343	        State::UpToDate | State::Ahead { .. } | State::NoRemote => Ok(()),
344	        State::Behind { .. } => {
345	            if args.no_check {
346	                info!("{}: fast-forwarding '{}'", repo.display(), args.bookmark);
347	                run(
348	                    "jj",
349	                    &[
350	                        "bookmark",
351	                        "set",
352	                        &args.bookmark,
353	                        "-r",
354	                        &remote_rev,
355	                        "-R",
356	                        &repo_str(repo),
357	                    ],
358	                    Path::new("."),
359	                )?;
360	            }
361	            Ok(())
362	        }
363	        State::Diverged { local, remote } => {
364	            if args.no_check {
365	                // `local` is either a single commit id or a comma-joined list
366	                // of heads when the bookmark is conflicted. Pick the head
367	                // that isn't the remote — that's the local-only tip. The
368	                // comma-joined path covers the jj post-fetch divergence
369	                // shape (local bookmark conflicted between old local head
370	                // and freshly-fetched remote head).
371	                let local_head = local
372	                    .split(',')
373	                    .find(|h| *h != remote)
374	                    .unwrap_or(local.as_str());
375	                info!(
376	                    "{}: rebasing {local_head} onto {remote_rev}",
377	                    repo.display()
378	                );
379	                run(
380	                    "jj",
381	                    &[
382	                        "rebase",
383	                        "-b",
384	                        local_head,
385	                        "-d",
386	                        &remote_rev,
387	                        "-R",
388	                        &repo_str(repo),
389	                    ],
390	                    Path::new("."),
391	                )?;
392	                if has_conflicts(repo)? {
393	                    return Err(format!("{}: rebase produced conflicts", repo.display()).into());
394	                }
395	            }
396	            Ok(())
397	        }
398	    }
399	}
400	
401	/// Print a single-line summary of `state` at `info!` level.
402	fn log_state(repo: &Path, state: &State) {
403	    let r = repo.display();
404	    match state {
405	        State::UpToDate => info!("{r}: up-to-date"),
406	        State::NoRemote => info!("{r}: no remote counterpart — skipping"),
407	        State::Ahead { local, remote } => {
408	            info!("{r}: ahead (local {local} > remote {remote}); nothing to sync")
409	        }
410	        State::Behind { local, remote } => {
411	            info!("{r}: behind (local {local} < remote {remote}); would fast-forward")
412	        }
413	        State::Diverged { local, remote } => {
414	            info!("{r}: diverged (local {local} vs remote {remote}); would rebase")
415	        }
416	    }
417	}
418	
419	/// Return the id of the most recent operation on `repo`.
420	///
421	/// Used as the revert target on failure. Exposed at `pub(crate)` so
422	/// `push` (0.37.0-2+) can reuse the same snapshot pattern for its
423	/// commit-stage rollback without duplicating the jj invocation.
424	pub(crate) fn current_op_id(repo: &Path) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
425	    let out = run(
426	        "jj",
427	        &[
428	            "op",
429	            "log",
430	            "--no-graph",
431	            "-n",
432	            "1",
433	            "-T",
434	            "id.short(12)",
435	            "-R",
436	            &repo_str(repo),
437	        ],
438	        Path::new("."),
439	    )?;
440	    Ok(out.trim().to_string())
441	}
442	
443	/// Restore `repo` to the operation identified by `op_id`.
444	///
445	/// Thin wrapper around `jj op restore`. Called during the failure
446	/// revert path — drops the caller's returned stdout. Exposed at
447	/// `pub(crate)` so `push` reuses the same restore call.
448	pub(crate) fn op_restore(repo: &Path, op_id: &str) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
449	    run(
450	        "jj",
451	        &["op", "restore", op_id, "-R", &repo_str(repo)],
452	        Path::new("."),
453	    )?;
454	    Ok(())
455	}
456	
457	/// Classify the relationship between `bookmark` and `bookmark@remote`.
458	///
459	/// Uses `bookmarks(<b>)` rather than the bare name so a conflicted
460	/// bookmark (jj's representation of a diverged fetch) resolves to all
461	/// of its heads instead of erroring. When the set has multiple heads,
462	/// the bookmark is conflicted and the repo is `Diverged` by definition.
463	/// Otherwise we compare the single local head against the single
464	/// remote commit via two revset-ancestry probes.
465	///
466	/// Returns `NoRemote` when `<b>@<remote>` does not resolve — the caller
467	/// logs a skip and moves on.
468	fn classify(
469	    repo: &Path,
470	    bookmark: &str,
471	    remote: &str,
472	) -> Result<State, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
473	    let local_heads = local_bookmark_heads(repo, bookmark)?;
474	    let remote_rev = format!("{bookmark}@{remote}");
475	    let Some(remote) = try_commit_id(repo, &remote_rev)? else {
476	        return Ok(State::NoRemote);
477	    };
478	    if local_heads.is_empty() {
479	        return Err(format!("{}: bookmark '{bookmark}' does not exist", repo.display()).into());
480	    }
481	    if local_heads.len() > 1 {
482	        // Conflicted bookmark — jj's shape for post-fetch divergence.
483	        let local = local_heads.join(",");
484	        return Ok(State::Diverged { local, remote });
485	    }
486	    let local = local_heads.into_iter().next().unwrap(); // OK: `len() > 1` arm handled above, `is_empty()` handled above
487	    if local == remote {
488	        return Ok(State::UpToDate);
489	    }
490	    let local_is_anc = revset_nonempty(repo, &format!("{local}::{remote_rev}"))?;
491	    let remote_is_anc = revset_nonempty(repo, &format!("{remote_rev}::{local}"))?;
492	    Ok(match (local_is_anc, remote_is_anc) {
493	        (true, _) => State::Behind { local, remote },
494	        (false, true) => State::Ahead { local, remote },
495	        (false, false) => State::Diverged { local, remote },
496	    })
497	}
498	
499	/// Return all commit ids the `bookmark` currently points at.
500	///
501	/// Normally a one-element vector; two or more elements indicate a
502	/// conflicted bookmark (jj's representation of diverged post-fetch
503	/// state, where the local bookmark has multiple heads).
504	fn local_bookmark_heads(
505	    repo: &Path,
506	    bookmark: &str,
507	) -> Result<Vec<String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
508	    let out = run(
509	        "jj",
510	        &[
511	            "log",
512	            "-r",
513	            &format!("bookmarks(exact:{bookmark})"),
514	            "--no-graph",
515	            "-T",
516	            r#"commit_id.short(12) ++ "\n""#,
517	            "-R",
518	            &repo_str(repo),
519	        ],
520	        Path::new("."),
521	    )?;
522	    Ok(out
523	        .lines()
524	        .map(|l| l.trim().to_string())
525	        .filter(|l| !l.is_empty())
526	        .collect())
527	}
528	
529	/// Return the short commit id that `rev` resolves to in `repo`.
530	///
531	/// Errors when the revset is unresolvable or matches multiple heads
532	/// — callers that want to tolerate "revset doesn't resolve" should use
533	/// `try_commit_id`.
534	fn commit_id(repo: &Path, rev: &str) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
535	    let out = run(
536	        "jj",
537	        &[
538	            "log",
539	            "-r",
540	            rev,
541	            "--no-graph",
542	            "-T",
543	            "commit_id.short(12)",
544	            "-R",
545	            &repo_str(repo),
546	        ],
547	        Path::new("."),
548	    )?;
549	    Ok(out.trim().to_string())
550	}
551	
552	/// Like `commit_id`, but `Ok(None)` when the revset doesn't resolve.
553	///
554	/// jj reports missing revisions via stderr strings like
555	/// `Revision \`foo@origin\` doesn't exist` or `No such revision …`;
556	/// both get mapped to `Ok(None)` so callers can distinguish "missing"
557	/// from "other subprocess failure".
558	fn try_commit_id(repo: &Path, rev: &str) -> Result<Option<String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
559	    match commit_id(repo, rev) {
560	        Ok(id) if id.is_empty() => Ok(None),
561	        Ok(id) => Ok(Some(id)),
562	        Err(e) => {
563	            let msg = e.to_string();
564	            if msg.contains("doesn't exist") || msg.contains("No such revision") {
565	                Ok(None)
566	            } else {
567	                Err(e)
568	            }
569	        }
570	    }
571	}
572	
573	/// Return `true` when `revset` matches at least one commit in `repo`.
574	fn revset_nonempty(repo: &Path, revset: &str) -> Result<bool, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
575	    let out = run(
576	        "jj",
577	        &[
578	            "log",
579	            "-r",
580	            revset,
581	            "--no-graph",
582	            "-T",
583	            r#"commit_id.short() ++ "\n""#,
584	            "-R",
585	            &repo_str(repo),
586	        ],
587	        Path::new("."),
588	    )?;
589	    Ok(!out.trim().is_empty())
590	}
591	
592	/// Return `true` when `repo` has any conflicted commits.
593	fn has_conflicts(repo: &Path) -> Result<bool, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
594	    revset_nonempty(repo, "conflicts()")
595	}
596	
597	/// Convert a path to a `String` suitable for passing as a subprocess arg.
598	fn repo_str(p: &Path) -> String {
599	    p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()
600	}

1	//! Unit tests for the sync module.

3	use super::*;

6	/// Default flags: neither `--check` nor `--no-check` set (the
7	/// implicit default is check mode), bookmark "main", remote "origin",
8	/// no `-R` and no `--scope` (caller will resolve via the
9	/// workspace-default scope), `--quiet` off.
10	#[test]
11	fn parse_defaults() {
12	    use clap::Parser;
13	    #[derive(Parser)]
14	    struct Cli {
15	        #[command(flatten)]
16	        args: SyncArgs,
17	    }
18	    let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(["test"]).unwrap();
19	    assert!(!cli.args.check);
20	    assert!(!cli.args.no_check);
21	    assert!(!cli.args.quiet);
22	    assert_eq!(cli.args.bookmark, "main");
23	    assert_eq!(cli.args.remote, "origin");
24	    assert!(cli.args.scope.is_none());
25	}
26	
27	/// `-q` / `--quiet` CLI form is honored.
28	#[test]
29	fn parse_quiet_flag() {
30	    use clap::Parser;
31	    #[derive(Parser)]
32	    struct Cli {
33	        #[command(flatten)]
34	        args: SyncArgs,
35	    }
36	    let cli = Cli::try_parse_from(["test", "--quiet"]).unwrap();
37	    assert!(cli.args.quiet);
38	    let cli_short = Cli::try_parse_from(["test", "-q"]).unwrap();
39	    assert!(cli_short.args.quiet);
40	}
41

52	        "test",

106	/// Sync args with `--no-check` set (apply mode).
107	///
108	/// Integration tests pass explicit repo paths through `sync_repos`
109	/// directly, so `scope` stays `None` here and the CLI-side default
110	/// resolution is not exercised by this helper.
111	fn apply_args() -> SyncArgs {
112	    SyncArgs {
113	        check: false,
114	        no_check: true,
115	        quiet: false,
116	        bookmark: "main".to_string(),
117	        remote: "origin".to_string(),
118	        scope: None,
119	    }
120	}
121	
122	/// Add a local-only commit on `main` in `repo` (not pushed), then
123	/// restore `@` to an empty child so pre-flight still passes.
124	///
125	/// Sequence: write file → describe `@` → advance `main` to `@` →
126	/// create a fresh empty `@` above it.
127	fn add_local_commit(repo: &Path, file: &str, content: &str, msg: &str) {
128	    fs::write(repo.join(file), content).expect("write local file");
129	    jj(repo, &["describe", "@", "-m", msg]);
130	    jj(repo, &["bookmark", "set", "main", "-r", "@"]);
131	    jj(repo, &["new"]);
132	}
133	
134	/// Clone `remote_url` into `<base>/<work_name>`, add a commit, push it.

  "new_string": "//! The `sync` subcommand: fetch + classify + (optionally) rebase /\n//! fast-forward a workspace's repos against their remotes.\n//!\n//! Default is `--check` (fetch + report, error if any repo needs\n//! action); `--no-check` applies. On any failure every repo is\n//! reverted to its pre-sync op via `jj op restore`.\n//!\n//! `current_op_id` / `op_restore` are `pub(crate)` so `push` reuses\n//! the same snapshot-and-restore pattern.\n\nuse std::path::{Path, PathBuf};\nuse std::process::Command;\n\nuse clap::Args;\nuse log::{LevelFilter, debug, info, warn};\n\nuse crate::common::{default_scope, find_workspace_root, run, scope_to_repos};\nuse crate::context::Context;\nuse crate::scope::{Scope, parse_scope};"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/sync.rs",
  "old_string": "    #[arg(\n        short = 's',\n        long,\n        value_name = \"SCOPE\",\n        value_parser = parse_scope,\n        verbatim_doc_comment\n    )]\n    pub scope: Option<Scope>,\n}\n\n/// Resolve `args` into the concrete repo list `sync_repos` operates on.\n///\n/// Explicit `--scope` wins; otherwise resolves via `default_scope`\n/// against the discovered workspace root and cwd.\nfn resolve_args_to_repos(args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let workspace_root = find_workspace_root();\n    let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;\n    let scope = match &args.scope {\n        Some(s) => s.clone(),\n        None => default_scope(workspace_root.as_deref(), &cwd),\n    };\n    scope_to_repos(&scope, workspace_root.as_deref())\n}",
  "new_string": "    #[arg(\n        short = 's',\n        long,\n        value_name = \"SCOPE\",\n        value_parser = parse_scope,\n        verbatim_doc_comment\n    )]\n    pub scope: Option<Scope>,\n}\n\n/// Inputs to the sync op, flat, owned, clap-free.\n///\n/// - `quiet`: `-q` / `--quiet` — clamp output to `Warn` for the run.\n/// - `bookmark`: bookmark to sync in each repo (default `main`).\n/// - `remote`: remote to sync against (default `origin`).\n/// - `no_check`: `--no-check` — actually rebase / fast-forward\n///   (absent ⇒ check mode: fetch + report only). The `--check`\n///   flag is the explicit form of the default and carries no\n///   value of its own, so it isn't mirrored here.\n/// - `scope`: `--scope` parsed (None ⇒ resolve via the\n///   workspace-default scope at run time).\npub struct SyncParams {\n    pub quiet: bool,\n    pub bookmark: String,\n    pub remote: String,\n    pub no_check: bool,\n    pub scope: Option<Scope>,\n}\n\nimpl From<&SyncArgs> for SyncParams {\n    /// Convert clap-derived `SyncArgs` into the flat `SyncParams`.\n    /// `--check` is dropped — it's the explicit form of the\n    /// default; the op only ever consults `no_check`.\n    fn from(a: &SyncArgs) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            quiet: a.quiet,\n            bookmark: a.bookmark.clone(),\n            remote: a.remote.clone(),\n            no_check: a.no_check,\n            scope: a.scope.clone(),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Resolve `params` into the concrete repo list `sync_repos`\n/// operates on.\n///\n/// Explicit `--scope` wins; otherwise resolves via `default_scope`\n/// against the discovered workspace root and cwd.\nfn resolve_params_to_repos(\n    params: &SyncParams,\n) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let workspace_root = find_workspace_root();\n    let cwd = std::env::current_dir()?;\n    let scope = match &params.scope {\n        Some(s) => s.clone(),\n        None => default_scope(workspace_root.as_deref(), &cwd),\n    };\n    scope_to_repos(&scope, workspace_root.as_deref())\n}"
}

  "old_string": "/// CLI entry point for the `sync` subcommand.\n///\n/// Thin wrapper over `sync_repos` that resolves the `-R` flag into a\n/// concrete repo list (falling back to the dual-repo default) and\n/// forwards the rest of the args. Tests call `sync_repos` directly\n/// with absolute fixture paths.\n///\n/// When `--quiet` is set, the global log filter is temporarily clamped\n/// to `Warn` for the duration of the call and restored on return, so\n/// `info!` calls throughout sync (plus any subprocess-stderr routed\n/// through `common::run`) go dark. Errors still surface at `Warn` /\n/// `Error` so script callers don't lose diagnostics.\npub fn sync(args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let repos = resolve_args_to_repos(args)?;\n    if args.quiet {\n        let prev = log::max_level();\n        log::set_max_level(LevelFilter::Warn);\n        let result = sync_repos(&repos, args);\n        log::set_max_level(prev);\n        result\n    } else {\n        sync_repos(&repos, args)\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// CLI entry point for the `sync` subcommand.\n///\n/// Thin wrapper over `sync_repos` that resolves `--scope` into a\n/// concrete repo list (falling back to the workspace-default\n/// scope) and forwards the rest. Tests call `sync_repos` directly\n/// with absolute fixture paths. `ctx` is unused today — present\n/// for the uniform subcommand-layer signature.\n///\n/// When `--quiet` is set, the global log filter is temporarily\n/// clamped to `Warn` for the duration of the call and restored on\n/// return, so `info!` calls throughout sync (plus any\n/// subprocess-stderr routed through `common::run`) go dark. Errors\n/// still surface at `Warn` / `Error` so script callers don't lose\n/// diagnostics.\npub fn sync(_ctx: &Context, params: &SyncParams) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let repos = resolve_params_to_repos(params)?;\n    if params.quiet {\n        let prev = log::max_level();\n        log::set_max_level(LevelFilter::Warn);\n        let result = sync_repos(&repos, params);\n        log::set_max_level(prev);\n        result\n    } else {\n        sync_repos(&repos, params)\n    }\n}"
}

  "old_string": "pub fn sync_repos(repos: &[PathBuf], args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    debug!(\n        \"sync: enter (no_check={}, bookmark={}, remote={})\",\n        args.no_check, args.bookmark, args.remote\n    );\n\n    // Preflight: verify bookmark tracking on every repo before any\n    // fetch/rebase. Implements \"Non-tracking-remote bookmark detection\"\n    // — design at:\n    //   https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/blob/main/notes/chores-06.md#non-tracking-remote-bookmark-detection-design\n    for repo in repos {\n        crate::common::verify_tracking(repo, &args.bookmark)?;\n    }\n\n    let mut snapshots: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = Vec::new();\n    for repo in repos {\n        let op_id = current_op_id(repo)?;\n        debug!(\"{}: op snapshot = {op_id}\", repo.display());\n        snapshots.push((repo.clone(), op_id));\n    }\n\n    let result = run_plan(&snapshots, args);",
  "new_string": "pub fn sync_repos(\n    repos: &[PathBuf],\n    params: &SyncParams,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    debug!(\n        \"sync: enter (no_check={}, bookmark={}, remote={})\",\n        params.no_check, params.bookmark, params.remote\n    );\n\n    // Preflight: verify bookmark tracking on every repo before any\n    // fetch/rebase. Implements \"Non-tracking-remote bookmark detection\"\n    // — design at:\n    //   https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1/blob/main/notes/chores-06.md#non-tracking-remote-bookmark-detection-design\n    for repo in repos {\n        crate::common::verify_tracking(repo, &params.bookmark)?;\n    }\n\n    let mut snapshots: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = Vec::new();\n    for repo in repos {\n        let op_id = current_op_id(repo)?;\n        debug!(\"{}: op snapshot = {op_id}\", repo.display());\n        snapshots.push((repo.clone(), op_id));\n    }\n\n    let result = run_plan(&snapshots, params);"
}

**Changes (`0.48.0-3` — `sync` → Context + Params):**

- `src/sync.rs` — added the missing `//!` module docstring; `+ use crate::context::Context;`; new `SyncParams` flat struct (`quiet` / `bookmark` / `remote` / `no_check` / `scope`) + `impl From<&SyncArgs>` (drops `--check` — it's the explicit form of the default; the op only consults `no_check`); `&SyncArgs` → `&SyncParams` across `sync` (→ `(_ctx, params)`), `sync_repos`, `run_plan`, `act_on_state`, and `resolve_args_to_repos` → `resolve_params_to_repos`; refreshed `sync()`'s stale "`-R` flag" doc text → `--scope`. `ctx` unused — uniform-signature placeholder.
- `src/main.rs` — `Sync` dispatch arm builds `Context::load(cli.log)` + `SyncParams::from(&sync_args)`.
- `src/sync/integration_tests.rs` — `apply_args() -> SyncArgs` → `apply_params() -> SyncParams` (drops `check`); 8 call sites updated. (`sync/tests.rs` unchanged — pure clap-parse tests on `SyncArgs`.)
- `ARCHITECTURE.md` — `sync` → `done (0.48.0-3)` in both tables; Migration A intro reworded to point at the table (stops the per-step "remaining N" / done-list churn).

- `notes/todo.md` — ladder marker `-3` → `(done)`.
- `Cargo.lock` — version string.

No `notes/README.md` change — the `sync` CLI surface is unchanged.

Proposed commit message:

**Title:**
```
refactor: sync → Context + SyncParams (0.48.0-3)
```

**Body:**
```
Migration A step 3: port `sync` to the subcommand-layer
`fn x(ctx, params)` shape. First port where the args struct
threads into private helpers (sync_repos / run_plan / act_on_state
/ resolve). Mechanical; no behavior change.

- Cargo.toml: 0.48.0-2 → 0.48.0-3
- src/sync.rs: + //! docstring; + use Context; SyncParams + From<&SyncArgs> (drops --check); &SyncArgs → &SyncParams across sync(_ctx,params)/sync_repos/run_plan/act_on_state/resolve_*; sync() doc -R→--scope
- src/main.rs: Sync arm builds Context::load(cli.log) + SyncParams::from
- src/sync/integration_tests.rs: apply_args→apply_params (drops check)
- ARCHITECTURE.md: sync → done (0.48.0-3) in both tables; Migration A intro reworded to point at the table
- notes/chores-09.md: "sync → Context + SyncParams (0.48.0-3)" subsection
